# Platform Roadmap Archive -- verbatim per-iteration history

Moved out of `PLATFORM_ROADMAP.md` in iteration 122 because that file had grown to 312,487
chars and these 98 bullets were 88.8% of it, which every roadmap-reading stage paid for on
both the read and the rewrite. Each bullet below is BYTE-IDENTICAL to the line it replaced:
join the lines matching `^- \*\*iter (\d+) ` in file order with a newline and the sha256 is
`0fee18f4f5568de1404fe49786e1ec6a5648131fd2f698dabfa570b62dfaa904`. Never summarise, re-wrap or re-order these; append new ones at the end.

No role reads this file. The terse index in `PLATFORM_ROADMAP.md` is the live document.

- **iter 01 = item 9 (`foundry doctor`).** Re-ordered ahead of item 1 for the first increment: it is purely additive (a new CLI subcommand + pure helpers), touches none of the running-loop semantics (no change to iteration numbering, state layout, or the sentinel contract), and directly attacks the top unattended-run failure mode (shifts dying on battery / missing `uv` / agent CLI / unreachable remote). Establishes the safe, offline-testable increment pattern.

- **iter 02 = item 11, bite 1 of 2 (SPLIT as instructed above).** This bite is the purely-additive, offline-testable half: three backward-compatible `ProductConfig` fields (`postrelease_enabled`, `setup_cmd`, `smoke_cmd`) plus a dormant, fully unit-tested fresh-clone verification helper (`verify_fresh_clone` + the pure `postrelease_verdict` / `sha_matches` decision functions + a single `run_cmd` I/O seam). Nothing calls the helper from `run_iteration`/`run_continuous` yet, so it changes NO running-loop semantics (iteration numbering, state layout, `VERDICT:`/`RESULT:`/`ACTION:` sentinels all untouched) — resume-safe for a live loop, same additive pattern iter 01 proved.

- **iter 03 = item 11, bite 2 of 2 (DONE — completes item 11).** The running-loop-touching half: wired `verify_fresh_clone` into `run_iteration` after the `ACTION: PUSHED` ship branch as a DETERMINISTIC inline step `postrelease_step` (NOT an LLM agent role — bite 1 already built the mechanical verify, and the product quality bar demands deterministic + offline-testable), emit the new `POSTRELEASE: HEALTHY|BROKEN` sentinel to `state/iter-NN/postrelease.md`, create/clear the per-product `HOTFIX_NEEDED.md` flag (write on BROKEN, clear on genuine HEALTHY, untouched on infra-skip/disabled), formalize the revertable single-commit-message contract in `roles/final.md`, add the hotfix-first first-duty to `roles/pm.md`, and add the ARCHITECTURE.md §2 stage row + §3 gate-invariant extension. Resume-safe: purely additive to the post-push branch, gated by `postrelease_enabled` (default True), BROKEN never reverts and keeps `status==shipped` (fixed forward next iteration), and a long-lived `run_continuous` process only picks up the change on a clean restart — so it can never corrupt an in-flight iteration.

- **iter 05 = item 10 (structured JSON event log `events.jsonl`) — RETRY.** First built in iter 04; that iteration completed engineering but was ABANDONED/reverted at the reviewer boundary when an external public-release-scrubbing STOP landed two chore commits (`5505ad0`, `a75099e`) on `main` — a process interruption, NOT a feature defect (nothing failed review). Re-picked as the smallest SAFE additive increment still on the list: a brand-new per-product file (`events.jsonl`) written best-effort inside the single `log()` choke function, OFF the control path, introducing NO new sentinel and changing NONE of iteration numbering / `state/iter-NN` layout / the `VERDICT:`/`RESULT:`/`ACTION:`/`POSTRELEASE:` contract — resume-safe for a live loop (a running `run_continuous` holds the old `log()` in memory; the change activates only on a clean restart, then just appends to a fresh file). Reuses iter 04's validated design + engineer lessons (tz-aware ISO `ts`, reserved-key insertion order, `json.dumps(default=str)`, durable-write-first best-effort mirror). Deferred over items 1-8: items 1 (`prd.json`) and 4 (risk-split gate) touch dispatcher/gate control flow; items 2 (Patterns head) and 5 (size guard) are useful but lower product value than the machine-readable observability the VISION's unattended-run goal needs. NO migration note required (no sentinel / control-flow change); ARCHITECTURE.md §5 gains a one-line note on the new artifact.

- **iter 06 = item 8 (`scheduled` watchdog to resurrect the dispatcher).** Re-ordered to the front of the remaining list on value×safety×size. It is the SAFEST class of increment left: a brand-new standalone module (`watchdog.py`) that NOTHING imports, so it cannot regress a running loop, the dispatcher, `foundry.py`, or any invariant — even safer than iter 05's in-`foundry.py` helper. It is squarely on-mission (VISION: always-on "indefinitely, until told to stop"; ARCHITECTURE §3 Resilience has a real gap — nothing resurrects the dispatcher process after a crash/kill/restart) and the product's own CONTINUOUS.md already prescribes the exact design (lines 41-42 + the line-57 `pgrep -f dispatcher.py` liveness probe). Its two safety-critical guards — single-brain (never launch a second dispatcher) and STOP-respect (never resurrect a deliberately-stopped company) — are a pure decision function pinned by a black-box truth table; all I/O sits behind monkeypatchable seams, so it stays fully offline/deterministic-testable. Detection is a `pgrep`-style process scan (no PID-file), so `dispatcher.py`/`foundry.py` are NOT edited → purely additive, zero control-flow/sentinel/numbering/layout change → **NO migration note required** (a new standalone module is off the control path entirely). Deferred over the rest: items 1 (`prd.json`) and 4 (risk-split gate) touch dispatcher/gate control flow; item 6 (mutation testing) needs a network install and isn't offline-deterministic; items 2/5/7 are lower immediate product value than closing the 24/7-survival gap. **Next-highest value after this: item 2 (bound the ever-growing `LEARNINGS.md` into a pinned `## Patterns` head)** — at ~42KB it is now a live per-iteration context-budget drain for every fresh agent that reads it.

- **iter 07 = item 2, bite 1 of 2 (SPLIT — the pre-declared next-highest-value item).** Bounding the ~49KB `LEARNINGS.md` context drain. Split into an additive bite now + a wiring bite later, mirroring the endorsed item-11 split (iter 02 dormant helper → iter 03 wiring). **Bite 1 (this iter):** a PURE, offline-testable `learnings_digest(text, recent=12)` helper (pinned `## Patterns` head verbatim + only the N most-recent role-tagged lessons) + a `foundry learnings --config <cfg> [--recent N]` CLI that renders it + restructuring the live (gitignored) `_platform/LEARNINGS.md` into a `## Patterns` head (seeded with ~10 curated durable rules) over a `## Chronological lessons` tail + a one-line pm.md pointer at the head. It touches ZERO control flow (`build_prompt`/`run_iteration`/`run_continuous`/`run_stage` untouched), adds NO sentinel, and changes nothing about iteration numbering / `state/iter-NN` layout / the `VERDICT:`/`RESULT:`/`ACTION:`/`POSTRELEASE:` contract → **NO migration note required**; a live loop is unaffected (old code in memory; CLI is purely additive on a clean restart). **Bite 2 (future):** wire `learnings_digest` into `build_prompt` so every stage receives the bounded digest inline. Deferred over items 1 (`prd.json`) and 4 (risk-split gate), which touch dispatcher/gate control flow, and item 6 (mutation testing), which needs a network install and isn't offline-deterministic; higher value than items 3/5/7 because the context drain hits EVERY agent EVERY iteration. **Next-highest after this: item 2 bite 2 (wire the digest into `build_prompt`)**, then item 3 (emit `AGENTS.md` per product repo).

- **iter 08 = item 2, bite 2 of 2 (DONE — completes item 2).** The wiring bite the iter-07 split pre-declared. `learnings_digest` is now inlined into `build_prompt`, so EVERY stage prompt of EVERY iteration carries the bounded digest (pinned `## Patterns` head + the `PROMPT_LEARNINGS_RECENT` most-recent lessons) directly — a fresh agent gets high-signal history inline instead of reading the ~49 KB `LEARNINGS.md` off disk (or running `foundry learnings`). `build_prompt` reads `cfg.learnings` DEFENSIVELY (missing/unreadable → placeholder digest, never crashes the pipeline) and reads the `PROMPT_LEARNINGS_RECENT` bound at call time (patchable). Purely ADDITIVE to the prompt STRING: the append-path line is KEPT (agents still need the path to append their own lesson), no signature change, `run_iteration`/`run_continuous`/`run_stage` untouched → NO new sentinel; iteration numbering, `state/iter-NN` layout, and the `VERDICT:`/`RESULT:`/`ACTION:`/`POSTRELEASE:` contract all unchanged → **NO migration note required**. Resume-safe: a live loop holds the old `build_prompt` in memory; the change activates only on a clean restart. **Next-highest after this: item 3 (emit an up-to-date `AGENTS.md` into each product repo from its learnings)** — additive, offline-testable, on-mission (fresh agents auto-read house rules), and the pre-declared successor.

- **iter 09 = item 3, bite 1 of 2 (SPLIT — the pre-declared next-highest-value item).** Emitting an `AGENTS.md` house-rules file into a product repo from its learnings. Split like the endorsed item-11 (iter 02→03) and item-2 (iter 07→08) splits: an additive-mechanism bite now + a wiring bite later. **Bite 1 (this iter):** a PURE `render_agents_md(learnings_text, product_name, recent=12) -> str` helper (an auto-generated banner + the bounded `learnings_digest` embedded verbatim, so the house rules ARE the pinned `## Patterns` head + the N most-recent lessons) + a new on-demand `foundry agents --config <cfg> [--recent N] [--print]` CLI (`agents_cli`) that writes `<cfg.repo>/AGENTS.md` (or prints it). Same class as the shipped `foundry doctor` (iter 01) and `foundry learnings` (iter 07) subcommands: purely additive, offline-testable, and the pipeline NEVER calls it, so `build_prompt`/`run_iteration`/`run_continuous`/`run_stage` are untouched, NO sentinel is added, and iteration numbering / `state/iter-NN` layout / the `VERDICT:`/`RESULT:`/`ACTION:`/`POSTRELEASE:` contract are unchanged → **NO migration note required**; a live loop is unaffected (old code in memory; the subcommand exists only on a clean restart). It is NOT run inside this iteration's pipeline — invoking it would write `AGENTS.md` into the repo root (`cfg.repo` IS the foundry repo for `_platform`) and dirty the ship diff, so it stays on-demand and the ship diff remains exactly {foundry.py, README.md, roadmap edit, new test file}. **Bite 2 (future):** auto-refresh `<repo>/AGENTS.md` at ship time (final gate / post-release) so it stays current on every ship — that half touches control flow AND makes `AGENTS.md` a tracked, gate-committed file, so it earns its own iteration + migration note. Deferred over items 1 (`prd.json`) and 4 (risk-split gate), which touch dispatcher/gate control flow, and item 6 (mutation testing), which needs a network install and isn't offline-deterministic. **Next-highest after this: item 3 bite 2 (wire the AGENTS.md auto-refresh into the ship path)**, then item 5 (PM task-size self-check field — already practiced informally in recent specs) or item 4 (risk-split the final gate for test-only diffs).

- **iter 10 = item 5 (Task-size guard) — DONE, COMPLETES item 5.** The roadmap's own pre-declared alternative-next after item 3 bite 2 — chosen OVER item 3 bite 2 on safety: bite 2 auto-commits an `AGENTS.md` into the PUBLIC foundry repo on every ship (touches the final-gate/ship path AND makes `AGENTS.md` a tracked, gate-committed file), which iter-09's PM explicitly flagged as an operator decision, not a safe autonomous increment. Item 5's mechanism is naturally small and fully additive, so NO split was needed: a PURE `spec_lint(spec_text) -> SpecLint` scorer (required-section presence + char count + `## Expected Behaviors` count, verdict `OK`/`REVIEW` against patchable module thresholds `REQUIRED_SPEC_SECTIONS`/`SPEC_SIZE_WARN_CHARS`/`SPEC_MAX_BEHAVIORS`) + a new on-demand `foundry lint-spec --file <path>` CLI (`lint_spec_cli`, exit 0 ok / 1 incomplete-or-oversized / 2 file-not-found, writes nothing) + formalizing `## Size self-check` as a REQUIRED PM spec section in `roles/pm.md` (the item's literal 'done when'). Same purely-additive class as the shipped `foundry doctor` (iter 01), `foundry learnings` (iter 07), and `foundry agents` (iter 09) subcommands: the pipeline NEVER calls it, so `build_prompt`/`run_iteration`/`run_continuous`/`run_stage` are untouched, NO sentinel is added, and iteration numbering / `state/iter-NN` layout / the `VERDICT:`/`RESULT:`/`ACTION:`/`POSTRELEASE:` contract are unchanged → **NO migration note required**; a live loop is unaffected (old code in memory; the subcommand exists only on a clean restart). Directly attacks the foundry's #1 reliability failure mode (oversized iterations → engineer-stage timeouts, the repolens smell) with an OBJECTIVE, deterministic pre-flight guard instead of prose-only self-assessment. Ship diff = {`foundry.py`, `roles/pm.md`, `README.md`, roadmap edit, new `tests/test_iter10_behavior.py`}. **Deferred/next-highest after this:** item 3 bite 2 (auto-refresh `AGENTS.md` at ship time — gate-committed tracked file, earns operator sign-off + its own iteration + migration note), then item 4 (risk-split the final gate for test-only diffs) or item 1 (`prd.json` machine roadmap); item 6 (mutation testing) still deferred as it needs a network install and isn't offline-deterministic.

- **iter 11 = item 1, bite 1 of 2 (SPLIT — the roadmap's #1-value item, deferred every iteration until now because its full form touches dispatcher control flow).** Split on the SAME seam the roadmap used for item 11 (iter 02 dormant helper → iter 03 wiring), item 2 (iter 07 helper+CLI → iter 08 wiring), and item 3 (iter 09 helper+CLI → future wiring): an additive-mechanism bite now + a control-flow wiring bite later. **Bite 1 (this iter):** a PURE `prd_status(prd_text) -> PrdStatus` parser for a per-product `prd.json` machine roadmap (a JSON array of stories, or `{stories: [...]}`, each with a `passes` flag) + a frozen `PrdStatus` (fields `valid/total/passed/pending`, props `complete/summary`) + a new `ProductConfig.prd` field (default `<repo>/prd.json`, backward-compatible) + an on-demand `foundry prd --config <cfg>` CLI (`prd_status_cli`, exit 0 complete / 1 incomplete / 2 missing-or-invalid, writes NOTHING). Same purely-additive class as the shipped `doctor` (iter 01), `learnings` (iter 07), `agents` (iter 09), and `lint-spec` (iter 10) subcommands: the pipeline NEVER calls it, so `build_prompt`/`run_stage`/`run_iteration`/`run_continuous`/`dispatcher.py` are untouched, NO sentinel is added, and iteration numbering / `state/iter-NN` layout / the `VERDICT:`/`RESULT:`/`ACTION:`/`POSTRELEASE:` contract are unchanged → **NO migration note required**; a live loop is unaffected (old code in memory; the subcommand appears only on a clean restart). No `prd.json` is created this iter and the CLI is read-only, so the ship diff stays exactly {`foundry.py`, `README.md`, roadmap edit, new `tests/test_iter11_behavior.py`} and NO `.gitignore` entry is needed. Chosen OVER item 3 bite 2 (auto-refresh `AGENTS.md` at ship time — commits a tracked file into the PUBLIC repo on every ship; iter 09/10 both flagged it for operator sign-off) and OVER items 4/7 (both touch gate or run-path control flow); item 6 (mutation testing) still deferred (needs a network install, not offline-deterministic). **Bite 2 (future):** wire `prd_status` into the dispatcher for deterministic progress reporting / an automatic global stop (touches dispatcher control flow → own iteration + migration note). **Next-highest after this: item 1 bite 2**, then item 4 (risk-split the final gate for test-only diffs) or item 7 (per-iteration suite wall-time).

- **iter 12 = item 1, bite 2a of (now) 3 — the reporting half of the iter-11 "bite 2".** Chosen as the roadmap's pre-declared next-highest item. On inspection, the original "bite 2" bundled two changes of very different risk: (a) deterministic progress *reporting* and (b) an *automatic global stop*. Part (b) alters the dispatcher's loop-termination / resume semantics — exactly what the quality bar forbids editing while a loop is live — so bite 2 was SPLIT on the same seam the roadmap used for items 11/2/3: an additive half now (**bite 2a**), the control-flow half later (**bite 2b**). **Bite 2a (this iter):** a PURE `dispatch_progress_line(cfg) -> str | None` helper in `foundry.py` (reads `cfg.prd`, formats via the frozen bite-1 `prd_status`, returns a one-line "N/M stories pass" summary or `None` when there is no `prd.json`; NEVER raises; writes nothing) wired into `dispatcher.py`'s shift loop as a DIAGNOSTIC `dlog` line guarded by `if prog:`. This is the first-ever `dispatcher.py` edit, but it is provably resume-safe and dormant-until-data: no configured product has a `prd.json`, so `dispatch_progress_line` returns `None` for every team and the dispatcher's runtime output is byte-identical to today — even after a clean restart — and the hook only begins emitting once an operator adds a `prd.json`. It changes NO loop control flow (`run_iteration`/`run_continuous`/`run_stage`/`build_prompt` untouched; round-robin / STOP / max-shifts / `res["status"]` branching unchanged), adds NO sentinel, and does not alter iteration numbering / `state/iter-NN` layout / the `VERDICT:`/`RESULT:`/`ACTION:`/`POSTRELEASE:` contract → **NO formal migration note required**; a short transparency note is added under §6 Migration notes anyway because it is the first `dispatcher.py` touch. Same proven dormant-until-data class as the postrelease config fields (iter 02) and the `prd_status` parser (iter 11). **Bite 2b (future, COMPLETES item 1):** make the dispatcher automatically retire a team / stop the company when its `prd` is complete — touches loop-termination / resume semantics → its own iteration + a real migration note. **Next-highest after this: item 1 bite 2b**, then item 4 (risk-split the final gate for test-only diffs) or item 7 (per-iteration suite wall-time); item 3 bite 2 (auto-refresh `AGENTS.md` at ship time) stays deferred for operator sign-off (commits a tracked file to the public repo), and item 6 (mutation testing) stays deferred (needs a network install, not offline-deterministic).

- **iter 13 = item 7, bite 1 of 2 (SPLIT — chosen OVER the pre-declared item 1 bite 2b on the standing value×safety×size filter).** Recording the fresh-clone test-suite wall-time per ship so unattended throughput becomes measurable. **Why not bite 2b (auto-stop when a `prd` is complete):** bite 2b changes the dispatcher's loop-termination / resume decision — exactly what the quality bar forbids editing while a loop may be live ("Never edit a currently-running loop's semantics in a way that breaks resume"); per the §6 self-mod guardrail it waits for its own iteration + a real migration note applied on a clean restart. **Why not item 4 (risk-split the gate):** the final gate is an LLM agent role, so a deterministic "test-only diff → light path" is lower-confidence and risks weakening the independent full-suite-rerun invariant — not a safe one-iteration additive ship. **Bite 1 (this iter):** a PURE `suite_timing_line(seconds, threshold) -> str` formatter + a patchable `SUITE_SLOW_SECONDS = 120.0` module constant + an optional inert `PostReleaseResult.test_seconds` field + timing the existing `run_cmd(cfg.test_cmd)` call inside `verify_fresh_clone` via a monkeypatchable `_monotonic` clock seam (bracketing ONLY the test command) + a guarded `postrelease_step` NIGHT_LOG/events line + a `suite_seconds` body line in `postrelease.md` (sentinel stays the last non-empty line). Same purely-additive observability class as `events.jsonl` (iter 05) and `foundry doctor` (iter 01): it rides the EXISTING post-release ship branch and the existing NIGHT_LOG/events.jsonl/postrelease.md artifacts, so it adds NO sentinel, needs NO new `.gitignore` entry, and touches NO control flow — `build_prompt`/`run_stage`/`run_iteration`/`run_continuous`/`dispatcher.py` are untouched, iteration numbering / `state/iter-NN` layout / the `VERDICT:`/`RESULT:`/`ACTION:`/`POSTRELEASE:` contract are unchanged → **NO migration note required**; a live loop is unaffected (old code in memory; the timing activates only on a clean restart, and only on ship iterations). **Bite 2 (future):** PM auto-files a speed story once a ship's suite exceeds `SUITE_SLOW_SECONDS`. **Next-highest after this: item 1 bite 2b** (dispatcher auto-stop on a complete `prd` — its own iteration + a real migration note), then item 4 (risk-split the final gate for test-only diffs); item 6 (mutation testing) stays deferred (needs a network install, not offline-deterministic), and item 3 bite 2 (auto-refresh `AGENTS.md` at ship time) stays deferred for operator sign-off (commits a tracked file to the public repo).

- **iter 14 = item 7, bite 2 of 2 (DONE — COMPLETES item 7).** The reactive half the iter-13 split pre-declared: surface the SLOW-suite signal to the next PM. Iter 13 made the per-ship fresh-clone suite wall-time measurable and logs a `SLOW` advisory past `SUITE_SLOW_SECONDS`, but that signal only lands in NIGHT_LOG/events — a fresh single-shot PM never reads it. This bite raises a per-product **`SPEED_STORY_NEEDED.md` advisory flag** on a genuine SLOW ship (mirroring the shipped `HOTFIX_NEEDED.md` lifecycle: write when `speed_story_needed(test_seconds, SUITE_SLOW_SECONDS)`, auto-clear on the next genuine fast ship, leave-untouched on infra-skip / disabled / errored where `test_seconds is None`), and adds an **advisory (NON-blocking)** PM duty in `roles/pm.md` right after the hotfix check. Key design calls: the advisory is keyed on the MEASURED wall-time vs threshold, INDEPENDENT of `healthy` (a slow suite is a throughput concern whether it passed or failed); it is NON-blocking and subordinate to `HOTFIX_NEEDED.md` (a present hotfix flag always wins); and its write/clear is SWALLOW-safe in `postrelease_step` (an informational advisory must never crash a shipped iteration) whereas the hotfix write stays UN-wrapped by design (a swallowed hotfix write would hide a real breakage). Same purely-additive, off-control-path, offline-testable class as the hotfix flag (iter 03) and the timing line (iter 13): it rides the EXISTING post-release ship branch, reuses the EXISTING patchable `SUITE_SLOW_SECONDS` + `PostReleaseResult.test_seconds`, adds NO new sentinel, and touches NO control flow (`build_prompt`/`run_stage`/`run_iteration`/`run_continuous`/`dispatcher.py` untouched); iteration numbering / `state/iter-NN` layout / the `VERDICT:`/`RESULT:`/`ACTION:`/`POSTRELEASE:` contract are unchanged. It adds a NEW per-product artifact (`SPEED_STORY_NEEDED.md`, git-ignored this iter) + an advisory PM duty, so a short transparency migration note is added (below + ARCHITECTURE.md §6) even though no sentinel changes — resume-safe (a live loop holds old code in memory; the change activates only on a clean restart, and only on ship iterations). Chosen OVER item 1 bite 2b (dispatcher auto-stop on a complete `prd` — touches loop-termination/resume semantics, its own iter + a real migration note), item 4 (risk-split gate — the final gate is an LLM role; a deterministic light path risks the independent full-suite-rerun invariant), item 6 (mutation testing — needs a network install, not offline-deterministic), and item 3 bite 2 (auto-refresh `AGENTS.md` at ship — commits a tracked file to the public repo, awaits operator sign-off). **Next-highest after this: item 1 bite 2b** (dispatcher auto-stop on a complete `prd`), then item 4; item 6 and item 3 bite 2 stay deferred as above.

- **iter 15 = item 4, bite 1 of 2 (SPLIT — the remaining safe additive item, chosen OVER the pre-declared item 1 bite 2b).** Item 4 (risk-split the final gate: test-only diff = light gate). Split on the SAME additive-mechanism-now / wire-later seam the roadmap used for items 11 (iter 02→03), 2 (iter 07→08), 3 (iter 09→…), and 1 (iter 11→12): a PURE, offline, repo-agnostic `classify_gate_scope(changed_paths) -> GateScope` classifier (buckets every changed path into test/doc/source; `light` = coverage-only = >=1 change AND every change is a test file — docs are conservatively NOT light because `roles/*.md` encode behavior) + a frozen `GateScope` (`changed/source/test/doc` + `light`/`scope` props) + two patchable module constants (`GATE_TEST_DIR_NAMES`, `GATE_DOC_SUFFIXES`) + a new on-demand `foundry gate-scope --config <cfg> [--base REF] [--files ...]` CLI (`gate_scope_cli`, exit 0 light / 1 full / 2 git-seam-error; obtains changed paths via `--files` or the EXISTING `run_cmd` git-diff seam; writes NOTHING). Same purely-additive, offline-testable, on-demand-CLI class as the shipped `doctor` (iter 01), `learnings` (iter 07), `agents` (iter 09), `lint-spec` (iter 10), and `prd` (iter 11) subcommands: the pipeline NEVER calls it, `build_prompt`/`run_stage`/`run_iteration`/`run_continuous`/`dispatcher.py` are untouched, NO sentinel / config field / artifact is added, and `roles/final.md` + the §3 independent full-suite-rerun invariant are UNCHANGED → NO `.gitignore` entry (writes nothing) and NO migration note required; a live loop is unaffected (old code in memory; the subcommand appears only on a clean restart). Ship diff = {`foundry.py`, `README.md`, roadmap edit, new `tests/test_iter15_behavior.py`}. **Chosen OVER item 1 bite 2b** (dispatcher auto-stop on a complete `prd`): its pure decision core already exists (`PrdStatus.complete` / `foundry prd`), so the only remaining work is WIRING a *new autonomous stop trigger* into the live shift loop — which both touches loop-termination/resume semantics (a loop is running now) AND conflicts with the operator's standing "run until EXPLICIT stop" directive → own iteration + migration note + operator sign-off. Item 3 bite 2 (auto-refresh `AGENTS.md` at ship — commits a tracked file to the PUBLIC repo) and item 6 (mutation testing — needs a network install, not offline-deterministic) stay deferred as before. **Bite 2 (future, COMPLETES item 4):** wire the `light` verdict into the final gate to skip the heavy path for coverage-only diffs WHILE preserving the §3 independent full-suite-rerun invariant — touches the gate/ship path → its own iteration + a migration note. **Next-highest after this: item 4 bite 2**, then item 1 bite 2b.

- **iter 16 = item 12 (NEW — read-only `foundry status` company-health probe; DONE, COMPLETES item 12).** With every safe roadmap increment shipped, all FOUR remaining items are legitimately blocked/deferred, so the PM exercised its roadmap-ownership to add and ship the highest value × safety × size increment available. **Why not the remaining items:** item 1 bite 2b (dispatcher auto-stop on a complete `prd`) — an automatic stop DIRECTLY conflicts with the operator's standing "run 24/7 until EXPLICIT stop" directive AND touches loop-termination/resume while a loop is live (own iter + migration note + operator sign-off); item 3 bite 2 (auto-refresh `AGENTS.md` at ship) — commits a tracked file to the PUBLIC repo, awaits operator sign-off since iter 09; item 4 bite 2 (wire the `light` gate path) — a DEEPER look than iter 15's defer: the §3 invariant REQUIRES the gate's full-suite rerun and item 11's post-release step re-runs the full suite on a FRESH clone to catch a new (test) file never `git add`-ed — a coverage-only diff is EXACTLY where that risk lives, so a light path would gut §3 or item 11; AND every `_platform` iteration so far changed `foundry.py` (source), so a coverage-only iteration essentially never fires → ~zero real latency savings for high risk = low value × high risk, defer (the iter-15 dormant classifier stays available for a future operator decision); - **iter 32 = HOTFIX (post-release BROKEN from iter 31) — make the fresh-clone suite green; NO roadmap feature.** `HOTFIX_NEEDED.md` was PRESENT (pushed sha `e3980d9`, 'fresh-clone test suite failed'), so per the hotfix-before-feature first duty the ONLY feature this iteration is the fix that makes post-release HEALTHY again. **Root cause (reproduced by cloning `origin/main` into a temp dir: `1 failed, 818 passed`):** iter-31's isolated tester wrote a live-smoke `tests/test_iter31_behavior.py::test_b13_live_smoke_on_real_dispatch_config` that hard-asserts `(froot / 'foundry.config.json').exists()`. That file is EXPLICITLY gitignored (`.gitignore:19` — 'the live dispatcher config is machine-local'), so it exists in the dev tree (final gate saw 819 passed) but is NEVER in a fresh clone → the post-release fresh-clone verify failed. This is exactly the dev-tree-leakage class item 11's post-release gate exists to catch (a green working tree is not a deployable release). **The fix (validated end-to-end in a real fresh clone before spec):** guard the live smoke to `pytest.skip(...)` when `foundry.config.json` is absent — the standard idiom for an environment-dependent smoke; preserves its value on a live machine (config present → RUNS → passes) while never failing a fresh clone / CI (config absent → SKIP → `818 passed, 1 skipped`, exit 0 → POSTRELEASE HEALTHY → flag auto-clears). NOT re-pointed at the tracked `foundry.config.example.json` (its `repolens` is `enabled: true`, contradicting the test's `repolens in disabled` assertion) and NOT rewritten to a synthetic config (the ~41 `_write_dispatch(tmp_path)` tests already give deterministic `company_history_cli` coverage — the live smoke is redundant when configless). This lands the offline, deterministic detector for that gap WITHOUT the network-install/non-determinism that keeps item 6-full (`mutmut`) blocked. **What ships (8 behaviors):** a PURE `find_constant_assert_tests(source) -> tuple[str,...]` (+ one small private classifier over `ast.walk(func)` computing `(has_constant_assert, has_real_signal)`), a structural MIRROR of the shipped iter-22 `_has_assertion_signal`/`find_assertionless_tests`: flags a `test*` def (top-level or class method, sync/async) iff its subtree has ≥1 constant `assert` (an `ast.Assert` whose `.test` is an `ast.Constant`) AND no real signal (a non-constant `assert`, an `ast.Raise`, an `assert*`-named call, or a `WEAK_TEST_ASSERTION_CALLS` call — read at call time so a monkeypatch bites); ascending source order; `SyntaxError` propagated verbatim; DISJOINT from `find_assertionless_tests` (a name is in at most one). REUSES the existing `import ast`, `_callee_trailing_name`, and the module global `WEAK_TEST_ASSERTION_CALLS` — NO new import / patchable constant / I/O seam / sentinel / config field / artifact / `.gitignore` entry / migration note. **Dormant + off the control path:** ZERO call site this iter (`run_iteration`/`run_continuous`/`run_stage`/`build_prompt`/`dispatcher.py`/`weak_tests_cli`/`find_assertionless_tests` all untouched); the shipped `find_assertionless_tests` and `foundry weak-tests`(`--json`) output stay byte-identical; `python -c "import foundry, dispatcher"` succeeds — the sanctioned additive-dormant pattern (a new module-level function with no caller; iter-02 `verify_fresh_clone` precedent), resume-safe for a live loop. Deliberately the LIGHTEST test-setup class on the board (source string in, name-tuple out — no monkeypatched `load_config`/subprocess/temp dispatch files, unlike the company-* roll-ups that stalled the tester at iter 45) to stay below the tester TTFT ceiling. **Bite 2 (future):** surface the findings to operators — e.g. fold a `constant_assert` finding category into the shipped `weak-tests`/`company-weak-tests` output (changes `WeakTestSummary`'s shape, consumed by the `company-weak-tests` family → its own iteration + a migration note) or ship a separate command. **Next-highest after this: item 6 offline slice bite 2** (wire the detector into a weak-tests finding category), then surface the four still-blocked items to the operator for a decision.

- **iter 110 = NEW reliability item R1 (IPC-self-healing), bite 1 of 2 = the DORMANT connect-probe resolver -- a VERBATIM RETRY of the iter-109 design (environmentally interrupted, not defective).** The desktop agent app rotates its IPC unix socket (`<runtime-dir>/<name>-<pid>.sock`) on every restart, but `run_stage` (foundry.py L10607) calls `subprocess.run(agent_cmd, ..., timeout=STAGE_TIMEOUT)` with NO `env=`, so every stage child inherits the launch-time (stale-after-restart) endpoint -> after a restart every stage instant-fails with the CLI help-text blob, the dispatcher stays alive-but-useless, and the loop stalls for hours with no watchdog and no human. This KILLED iter 108 (`state/iter-108/engineer.attempt1.log` = the exact 500B stale-IPC signature) and it is the operator's labelled HIGHEST-VALUE RELIABILITY PICK (2026-08-04). **What ships (purely additive to foundry.py; ZERO call site -- DORMANT, so the running loop is byte-identical and resume-safe):** a stdlib-only module-level pure `resolve_agent_endpoint(sock_dir=None) -> str | None` that globs `*-*.sock` (default from the `FOUNDRY_AGENT_SOCK_DIR` env var, overridable for tests), orders candidates NEWEST-mtime-first, and returns the FIRST that answers a real `socket.connect()` probe (~0.5s timeout, always closed in `finally`), else `None`; never raises on a missing/empty dir, malformed name, stat error, or probe error. The connect-probe is the ONLY liveness test -- NOT `os.kill(pid,0)` on the filename pid, which the operator TESTED on 2026-08-04 and found actively WRONG due to PID reuse (a stale `<name>-891.sock` had pid 891 reused by a live process -> `os.kill` reported EXISTS). **Triage:** RETRY of iter-109 per the retry rule -- iter 109 reverted with all four engineer attempts = 48-byte `agent run timed out after 600s` logs (a THROTTLE/TTFT stall, agent produced NOTHING), which is an ENVIRONMENTAL interruption, not a review defect, so the design is re-done VERBATIM. `rg resolve_agent_endpoint foundry.py dispatcher.py watchdog.py` = NOTHING confirms the feature is absent from the tree. Chosen (Scout B C1) OVER Scout A C1 (DIRECTIONS.md discovery bite 4): the reliability floor precedes the discovery-bite order when a live failure is eating iterations (HEAD frozen at iter-107's ship for TWO iters), same call as the iter-109 PM. **Out of scope / bite 2 of 2 (future):** wire the resolver into `run_stage` as `env={**os.environ, <AGENT_IPC_ENDPOINT_ENV>: resolved}` (only when a socket answered; `None` -> leave inherited env untouched) -- touches the live control path, earns its own iteration + migration note; and a `foundry doctor` stale-endpoint WARN (deferred -- doctor's 4-check contract is pinned by iter-01 tests). Ship set = {foundry.py, README.md (optional note), PLATFORM_ROADMAP.md, new tests/test_iter110_behavior.py}. 10 behaviors, offline, lint-spec OK.
