## Detailed spec — item 11: post-release verification gate + revertable-commit contract

Added 2026-07-30 from a sibling continuous factory (`~/projects/proactive-factory/`)
that has shipped 15 iterations to a PUBLIC repo with this gate green on every ship —
so the design below is live-validated, not theoretical. Direct this item's PM to it.

### Why (on-mission for the foundry's continuous-deployment goal)
The Final Reviewer proves the *working tree* is green. It does NOT prove the *pushed
commit* is deployable. The gap is exactly the class of bugs that break real releases:
a file created but never `git add`-ed, `uv.lock` drift, an import that only resolves
because of leftover dev-tree state. A CD system's whole promise is "what landed on the
branch actually works from a clean checkout." This stage closes that gap.

### What the stage does (additive; slots in AFTER the `ACTION: PUSHED` branch)
Only runs when the final gate actually pushed (`push_enabled` True and remote head moved):
1. Clone `origin/<branch>` fresh, shallow, into the iteration state dir
   (a clean room — NOT the dev working tree).
2. Run, in the clone: `cfg.setup_cmd` (default `uv sync`) → `cfg.test_cmd` (full suite)
   → `cfg.smoke_cmd` (optional per-product demo/smoke, e.g. `make demo`; skip if unset).
3. Confirm the cloned HEAD sha == the sha the final gate reported.
4. Delete the throwaway clone.
5. Emit a new sentinel as the output file's final line:
   `POSTRELEASE: HEALTHY` / `POSTRELEASE: BROKEN`.

### Failure handling
- BROKEN → write `products/<name>/HOTFIX_NEEDED.md` (with the sha + verbatim evidence).
  The next iteration's PM MUST prioritize a hotfix over any new feature. HEALTHY on a
  later iteration clears the flag. The gate does NOT auto-fix and NEVER force-pushes —
  a bad public commit is fixed forward by the next iteration, not rewritten.
- Infra tolerance: a network failure during `git clone`/`uv sync` is INFRA, not a broken
  release. In that case emit `POSTRELEASE: HEALTHY` (verification skipped, note why) so a
  transient network blip never raises a false hotfix.

### Revertable-commit contract (the second half of this item)
Make the final gate's commit message a documented contract, not a convention:
`<type>: <summary> (foundry <product> iter NN)` where type ∈ feat/fix/chore/docs/test.
Every release is then greppable and single-commit-revertable — which is what makes
"fix forward, or revert one commit" a safe operation for a public repo.

### Config additions (default-on, backward compatible)
- `postrelease_enabled: bool = True`
- `setup_cmd: str = "uv sync"`, `smoke_cmd: str | None = None` (per product)
- All optional with safe defaults so existing product configs keep working.

### Invariant compliance (read §3 + the self-mod guardrails above)
- Purely ADDITIVE: runs only after a successful ship, so it does NOT change iteration
  numbering, state layout, or the `VERDICT:`/`RESULT:`/`ACTION:` sentinels → resume-safe
  for any loop already in flight.
- Introduces ONE new sentinel (`POSTRELEASE:`). When you implement, record it in
  ARCHITECTURE.md §2 (add the stage row) and §3 (extend the gate invariant) with a
  migration note here, per the self-modification guardrails.

### Reference implementation (copy/adapt, don't reinvent)
- Role playbook: `~/projects/proactive-factory/roles/postrelease.md` (the exact clone /
  setup / test / smoke / sha-match / delete / sentinel + infra-tolerance rules).
- Orchestration wiring: `~/projects/proactive-factory/factory_pla.py`
  → `run_pipeline_tail()` (the post-release branch after `ACTION: PUSHED`, plus the
  `HOTFIX_NEEDED.md` create-on-BROKEN / clear-on-HEALTHY logic).

### Done when
- [ ] A `postrelease` role + stage exist; stage runs on every ship, skipped on no-ship.
- [ ] Verifies on a FRESH clone (setup + full suite + optional smoke), matches the sha.
- [ ] Emits `POSTRELEASE: HEALTHY|BROKEN`; BROKEN writes the per-product hotfix flag.
- [ ] Network failure during clone/sync degrades to HEALTHY-skipped (no false hotfix).
- [ ] Final-gate commit-message contract documented and enforced in `roles/final.md`.
- [ ] ARCHITECTURE.md §2/§3 updated + migration note; `tests/` stay green; both modules
      still import.

---

- **iter 139 -- the two GATE role cards now carry the verify-first EXCEPTION their own stages need, and a new pure `write_early_card_audit` plus a LIVE suite assertion makes losing it impossible (scout A's hardening/DX lens, candidate A1, CORRECTED after measurement).** The defect: `roles/final.md` and `roles/tester.md` carried the unqualified iter-112 WRITE-EARLY block -- "write a complete-but-minimal version of your required output file AS SOON AS your decision is made" -- while the operator head inlined into every stage prompt told those two stages the OPPOSITE, and neither exception appeared in either card. For the gate stage the card was ordering an unconditional ship blocker: `parse_ship_action` (foundry.py:8106) recognises ONLY `ACTION: PUSHED <sha>` and `ACTION: REVERTED`, so a placeholder `ACTION: PENDING` is unrecognised and `run_iteration` reverts. Re-verified this iteration on disk, four losses, each with a 48-byte `final.attempt1.log` (i.e. killed AFTER its own gates went green): _platform iter-118 `ACTION: PENDING (checkpoint written before commit)`, iter-120 `ACTION: REVERTED`, iter-123 `ACTION: PENDING`, repolens iter-11 `ACTION: REVERTED`. TWO scout claims were checked rather than inherited. (1) Scout A's own strongest objection said the losses happened while the correct rule was already in the prompt head; FALSE for the exception -- the operator's FINAL-STAGE EXCEPTION is dated 2026-08-06 and cites those four as its proof, and the artifacts' mtimes are 2026-08-05 (118, 120) and 2026-08-06 (123, repolens 11), so the cards have never carried it and this is not a third copy of a failed rule. (2) The head's TESTER directive ("never a placeholder RESULT: FAIL, an unconditional ship blocker") is OVER-BROAD and was deliberately NOT copied: iteration 126 shipped the mechanism that makes a MARKED tester checkpoint safe -- `classify_test_report` (foundry.py:8746) returns UNFINISHED when the body carries `UNFINISHED_TEST_MARKER = "PROGRESS: CHECKPOINT"` (foundry.py:8725) and RED only otherwise, and `roles/tester.md` duty 3 already mandates that marker -- so copying the head verbatim would have contradicted a shipped design and told the tester to stop checkpointing. The two cards therefore received DIFFERENT exceptions by measurement: final.md must write the `ACTION:` line ONCE after the decision is real, never as a placeholder; tester.md may checkpoint but a cut-short report MUST carry `PROGRESS: CHECKPOINT` verbatim (an UNMARKED `RESULT: FAIL` is classified RED and burns the single repair round on a `fix-tests` pass with nothing to fix) and every checkpointed claim must be measured rather than predicted (the iter-138 lesson). Shape: prose additions inside each card's existing WRITE-EARLY section, keeping both anchors `WRITE-EARLY (checkpoint-first)` and `write a complete-but-minimal version` verbatim in all 8 top-level cards (`tests/test_iter112_behavior.py` pins them, including a marker-count-equals-card-count test, so rewording either turns the suite RED), plus four module-level constants (`WRITE_EARLY_MARKER`, `WRITE_EARLY_MECHANISM`, `WRITE_EARLY_EXCEPTION_ANCHOR` = `EXCEPTION for this card`, `SENTINEL_VERDICT_CARDS` = `("final.md", "tester.md")`), one frozen `WriteEarlyAudit` record (`missing_marker` / `missing_mechanism` / `missing_exception` / stored `ok`) and one pure total `write_early_card_audit(cards)` that reads those globals at call time -- the same DORMANT pure-function-plus-suite-assertion shape iteration 133 used for `scout_lens_audit`, with ZERO call site in the pipeline, so an in-flight loop's resume semantics are byte-identical. The CONSUMER is a live suite assertion over the 8 real cards read off disk, not a report: deleting a card or dropping either gate card's exception fails the ship. Scout B's A1 (inject a resolving `python3 <FOUNDRY>/foundry.py <verb>` line into every stage prompt, closing item (h)) was the strongest alternative and was rejected for this iteration because it saves a stage TURN where this prevents LOST ITERATIONS, and because it would edit the hot `build_prompt` path; it needs its own oracle (verify the command resolves from a NON-foundry cwd) and stays the next candidate for that lens. ALSO in this commit, the first half of roadmap item (n): the index was 395 chars from the hard 60,000 `ROADMAP_SIZE_WARN_CHARS` ship brake (measured 59,605 CHARS, not the 59,811 BYTES `wc -c` reports -- the brake counts `len(text)` and the index holds 206 bytes of multibyte punctuation), which would have made iteration 141's own Done-ledger row unshippable, so the COMPLETED item-11 detailed spec (4,234 chars, shipped iters 02/03) moved VERBATIM to this archive under `## Moved from the index by iter 139`, leaving a pointer and taking the index to 56,270 chars. `roadmap_archive_gaps` is one-directional (it only reads `- **iter N ` bullets and never reports archive-only numbers), so appending non-iteration prose here is safe; the `## Item 16` section (3,467 chars) is the next target once its ship status is confirmed.

## Item 16 — committed, portable pre-push leak-guard (HIGH: repo is public + auto-pushing)

**Problem.** This repo is public and the dispatcher auto-pushes on every successful ship
with NO human review in the loop. A drifted iteration can reintroduce sensitive strings
(the internal agent-CLI tool name, the model-provider service name, internal skill and
workflow names, internal credential-refresh command names, personal absolute home-directory
paths, and personal usernames) directly into a public commit. A local `.git/hooks/pre-push`
guard is installed today, but git hooks are NOT cloned — so a fresh checkout (including the
post-release fresh-clone verify, a new operator, or CI) ships with ZERO protection.

**What ships.**
- `scripts/leak_guard.py` — a stdlib-only, offline, deterministic scanner. Given a commit
  ref (or an explicit file list), it scans tracked blob content against a configurable
  denylist of *token-aware* patterns (each pattern flanked so ordinary English words that
  merely contain the fragment are never false-positives). Exits non-zero with a `file:line`
  report on any hit. Its OWN path is excluded from the scan so it can never self-trip on the
  denylist literals it necessarily contains.
- `scripts/install_hooks.sh` — one command a fresh clone runs to copy/symlink the scanner
  into `.git/hooks/pre-push` (documented in README setup).
- Final-gate integration — the `final` role runs the scanner as a hard pre-commit/pre-push
  check so the loop self-blocks a leaky ship even when the hook is not installed
  (belt-and-suspenders). A blocked ship fails the gate and reverts, same as any other gate
  failure.
- The denylist lives in a small committed config (e.g. `scripts/leak_denylist.txt`) so it is
  reviewable and extensible without editing code.

**Design / invariant compliance (read §3 + the self-mod guardrails).**
- Purely ADDITIVE and offline-deterministic (no network) — fits the test-speed + offline-CI
  invariants; does not change iteration numbering, state layout, or the
  `VERDICT:`/`RESULT:`/`ACTION:`/`POSTRELEASE:` sentinels → resume-safe for any loop in flight.
- Token-aware matching is mandatory (word-boundary / non-letter flanks) to avoid blocking
  legitimate prose the autonomous roles write every iteration.

**Done when.**
- [x] `scripts/leak_guard.py` exists, offline, stdlib-only, with unit tests covering: a clean
      tree passes; each denylist category is caught; a benign word containing a fragment is
      NOT flagged; the guard's own path is skipped. **(iters 49-50)**
- [x] `scripts/install_hooks.sh` arms the pre-push hook in one command; README documents it. **(iter 51)**
- [x] The `final` role invokes the scanner before pushing and treats a hit as a gate failure. **(iter 52)**
- [x] `tests/` stay green; both modules still import; ARCHITECTURE.md notes the new gate step. **(iter 52)**

- **iter 140 -- `foundry lint-config` now RECOGNISES a dispatcher roster and exits 2 instead of telling the
  operator to delete their own company (scout A's integration-and-adoption lens, candidate A2 in its small
  variant).** Measured in the PM stage: `python3 foundry.py lint-config --config foundry.config.json` -- the
  live roster that decides which teams get a shift -- exited 1 with `[error] work_items: unknown config key
  'work_items'; not a ProductConfig field -- prefix it with '_' to keep it a comment`. Obeying that advice is
  destructive: `dispatcher.py:89` reads `conf.get("work_items", [])`, so a `_work_items` roster yields zero
  items and `dispatcher.py:91` prints "No enabled work items in config." and exits -- with the watchdog
  relaunching it to the same exit all night. The roster is exactly the file the operator hand-edits, since
  iteration 137's `new-product` PRINTS a paste-ready roster snippet and leaves the paste to the human. Shape:
  one pure module-level `dispatch_roster_note(raw)` returning None for a product config and, for a mapping
  whose top-level `work_items` is a list, an operator sentence naming the roster plus every product config it
  lists; `lint_config_cli` consults it by BARE module name inside its existing `ConfigKeyError` handler,
  BEFORE `config_key_findings`, and on a note prints it and returns 2 with a 4-key `--json` document
  (`config_path`, `error`, `exit_code`, `kind`: `dispatch_roster`) whose first three keys stay in the order
  the iteration-134 exit-2 document uses. Fail-safe in the iteration-134 style: if the seam returns None or
  raises, the input keeps its previous behavior exactly, so the branch can never make the old path worse.
  `_report_unreadable_config` was left untouched on purpose -- `tests/test_iter134_behavior.py` pins its
  document to exactly `("config_path", "error", "exit_code")`, so a missing or corrupt file still reports
  the historical 3-key shape. DELIBERATELY NOT taken, and left as named follow-ons: a 45th `lint-roster` verb
  and scout A2's five roster CONTENT checks (duplicate names, unresolvable `config` paths, all-items-disabled,
  an unrostered `products/*/config.json`) -- scout A's own census, 44 verbs with only 3 named by any automated
  consumer, is the argument against adding a verb to fix an adoption gap. Also NOT taken: scout B's B1
  (collapse the nine `company_*_cli` bodies, 547 lines, roadmap item (i)), because it is this product's first
  non-additive refactor of shipped code and wants a clean iteration; and scout B's B2 as a FEATURE (index
  compaction), because this file's own DEADLINE block calls compaction "a scheduling item, never a
  competitive candidate" -- so iteration 140 did it as PM maintenance in this same commit instead.

- **iter 141 -- the MECHANISM (SHIPPED DORMANT -- no call site, see the BLOCKER at the end of this bullet) for a `products/<name>/RESTART_NEEDED.md` operator flag carrying the live-lag line, so "shipped but not live" surfaces without anyone remembering to run `foundry doctor` (scout B's performance-and-throughput lens, candidate B1).** The measured problem, proven from the live brain's OWN output rather than from the checker that reports it: `foundry live-lag` named 19 shipped-but-inert iterations (118, 119, 122, 124, 126-140), and iteration 129 -- committed 08-07 07:27 -- makes every retry line print a `(failure kind: ...)` suffix, yet `dispatcher.out` contains ZERO such lines and the two most recent backoff events (08-08 23:24:52, 08-09 00:10:00) still print the pre-129 shape `backing off 10 min`. So the loop was executing 3-day-old code. Cost: 1,050 min = 17.5 h of retry sleep paid at a ladder whose replacement had already shipped, 220 min of it in the two days AFTER the fix landed (9 of those 13 events were `tester` stages, the exact cap-timeout/stall kinds whose shipped delay is `RETRY_DELAY_FLOOR = 60`s). The root cause is structural, not a one-off: `dispatcher.py` does a plain `import foundry` ONCE at launch, so on a long-running process EVERY foundry improvement is dormant -- iteration 130 shipped the detector for exactly this and gave it only one PEDAL, a `doctor` line the operator runs BEFORE a launch, which by construction cannot reach an operator who has already launched. Shape: `RESTART_FLAG_NAME` + a pure `cfg`-only path helper + one write seam + one clear seam + a `dispatch_restart_line(cfg)` reporter modelled exactly on iter-12's `dispatch_progress_line` (composes `live_lag_line` by BARE module name so a `monkeypatch.setattr` bites; returns the WARN line or `None`; never raises), INTENDED as ONE guarded call in the dispatcher shift loop but NOT LANDED (see BLOCKER below), with the flag gitignored in the SAME iteration via a NEW nested `products/.gitignore` (`*/RESTART_NEEDED.md`) because the ROOT `.gitignore` is frozen too. The flag AUTO-CLEARS on the OK/UNKNOWN branches so it can never nag after the restart it asked for. Deliberately NOT taken: any auto-`execv`/`importlib.reload` self-restart (acting on the flag stays a HUMAN decision -- swapping the semantics of a mid-shift loop is what the quality bar forbids, and a crash-loop stops the whole company), no role card reads the flag (unlike `HOTFIX_NEEDED.md` it must never gate or redirect a stage), and `live_lag_line` / `live-lag` / `doctor` semantics are untouched. Also NOT taken, with the reasons recorded so they are not re-litigated: scout A's A2 (collapse the seven byte-identical `summarize_company_*` constructors, 135 LOC, an AST census over all 222 module-level functions proved them identical and the 7 target dataclasses uniform, 0 `monkeypatch.setattr` sites, est. -116 LOC) -- it remains the recommended ON-RAMP to item (i) and strictly shrinks it, but it is this product's first non-additive refactor of shipped code and changes no decision; scout A's A3 (the STATUS stack) -- partially taken as PM maintenance, see `## Moved from the index by iter 141`; scout B's B2 (concurrent scout stages, a measured 476s = 14.1% of a 3,388s median iteration) -- two concurrent agent-CLI children draw on ONE token budget while 28% of produced attempts already finish at/over the 600s cap, so it is the only candidate that can make throughput WORSE; scout B's B3 (`stage-times --last N` windowing) -- cheapest and safest, and the instrument B2 needs before anyone wires concurrency on, so it is the standing next candidate for that lens. Four throughput stories were also KILLED WITH NUMBERS by scout B so no future scout re-proposes them for THIS product: the full suite averages 33.68s over iters 132-140 (the standing per-product FAST-test-command directive is worth ~2% here and stays correct only for a slow product like repolens at 498s), `LEARNINGS.md` is 2.88 MB but `read_text()` is 1.2 ms (the per-stage learnings cost is prompt SIZE, not I/O -- 16,223 of 18,243 prompt chars, with the pinned head at 92% of its 10,000-char budget and therefore one long bullet from silent truncation), dispatcher hand-off between teams is 1 second, and post-release clone+deps is ~3s of a 39s window. BLOCKER, measured in the engineer stage and re-verified independently by the reviewer: 26 permanent every-suite guards in `tests/test_iter50..84_behavior.py` each assert `git diff --quiet HEAD -- dispatcher.py scripts/ .gitignore`, so ANY edit to `dispatcher.py` or the root `.gitignore` turns 26 tests RED at this iteration's own pre-commit gate (probe: append one comment line to `dispatcher.py`, run `pytest -q tests -k byte_unchanged` -> 26 failed, 3 passed in 1.3s). `tests/test_control_path_freeze_scope.py` pins them in place (guard-count FLOOR of 26; every guard must keep naming `dispatcher.py`), so the correct fix is TEST-OWNED and cannot be made from the engineer chair. CONSEQUENCE for every future PM: no dispatcher-side pedal -- this one or any other -- is shippable until a dedicated iteration re-scopes those 26 byte-freezes to an AST/symbol invariant (dispatcher.py still imports foundry, still calls exactly load_config / run_iteration / dispatch_progress_line in the shift loop, still honors both STOP files). Do NOT spec another `dispatcher.py` edit before that unblocking iteration exists; it will fail the same way, and the one-line wiring that activates this flag is the first thing it should unblock.
- **iter 142 -- `roles/pm.md`'s two bare `foundry ...` invocations become a form that actually RUNS, plus a two-sided suite brake so no card can name an unrunnable one again (scout B's narrative-and-docs lens, candidate B1; retires open roadmap item (h) and the operator's pinned PAPER-CUT).** Measured first-hand in the PM stage rather than inherited: `command -v foundry` returns nothing, `pyproject.toml` declares no `[project.scripts]` console script, and exactly 2 of the 16 `foundry <word>` lines under `roles/` are command positions -- `roles/pm.md:29` (`foundry learnings`) and `roles/pm.md:84` (`foundry lint-spec --file <path to this pm.md>`), the latter inside the REQUIRED `## Size self-check`. So every PM stage in the fleet was ordered to run a command that has never existed, in the stage with the least remaining budget; the operator's note records iteration 92 burning a turn on exactly this and falling back to calling `spec_lint` directly. The replacement was VERIFIED BEFORE being specced, which is the only reason it is not a second broken command: `python3 <checkout>/foundry.py lint-spec --file <spec>` returned a real verdict both from the repo root and from `/tmp` with absolute paths, on plain `python3` with no `uv` and no third-party dependency. Second defect on the same line, fixed with it: `<path to this pm.md>` is AMBIGUOUS because the PM's output spec and the PM's role card are both named `pm.md`, and linting the card yields a plausible-looking `missing sections: ## Feature, ## Why, ...` failure about the wrong document. THE MEASUREMENT THAT MAKES THE BRAKE HONEST, and the reason this iteration is not a fail-open detector: the CLI has 44 verbs, not the 41 a scraper finds, because `run`, `once` and `doctor` are built by a `for name in ("run", "once", "doctor")` loop at `foundry.py:13893` instead of `add_parser("<literal>")` -- so a literal-only verb set silently MISSES `doctor`, which is precisely the positive control a tester reaches for. The spec therefore pins both construction shapes and REQUIRES `doctor` as the planted positive control, with the 14 real prose lines (`- The foundry learnings log.`, the `(foundry iter NN)` commit tag, `the foundry knows how to staff`) as the negative control, and a non-vacuity floor (>= 8 cards read, >= 40 verbs derived) so an empty glob cannot report green. Shape: two pure functions (`foundry_cli_verbs(source_text)`, `bare_foundry_cli_findings(text, verbs)`), one new test module, two corrected prose lines; no config key, no runtime artifact, no `dispatcher.py`/`scripts/`/`.gitignore` edit. Shippability was checked FIRST, and is the opposite of 141's: the freeze guards cover only four pathspec sets and `tests/test_control_path_freeze_scope.py:55` declares `FORBIDDEN = ("README.md", "roles/")`, so a guard freezing a role card would itself redden the suite -- `roles/*.md` is always editable. NOT TAKEN, with reasons recorded so they are not re-litigated: scout A's A1 (optional `fast_test_cmd`, a real 2.85x measured twice -- 48.57s serial vs 17.05s under `-n auto`, 4149 passed both runs, tree clean -- worth ~165s of the ~259s/iteration spent on ~5.3 in-stage suite runs) was rejected on THREE grounds, not on size: it is additive-DORMANT so it delivers 0 seconds until some config sets the field; its enabling mechanism puts `-n auto` and ~10 workers against ONE working tree while 26 every-suite guards shell `git diff --quiet HEAD`, and `.git/index.lock` contention would land the flake on `engineer`, the stage already AT the 600s cap in all three products (20 of the fleet's 72 timeouts) where a false red costs a whole shift; and its shape was de-listed by iteration 126 with roadmap item (k) blocked, so it carries live reviewer-rejection risk. It stays a good candidate once a tester has proven the parallel run against those guards more than twice. Scout A's A2 (bounded `roadmap_open_items` view) -- a prose parser that can silently return a WRONG or empty view, strictly worse than the full read, and it buys tokens rather than seconds. Scout A's A3 (stream the child's stdout so a timed-out attempt keeps its transcript instead of a 48-byte log; 12.00 h of fleet time destroyed) -- the biggest raw number available and still the standing candidate for that lens, but it rewrites `run_stage`, the hottest control path, where a streaming rewrite disturbs the exit-code and timeout handling every retry depends on. Scout B's B2 (item (l)) and B3 (item (m)) -- both remain listed and unblocked. Iteration 141's own recommendation of the 26-guard re-scope as the 142 feature was DECLINED: neither scout proposed it, the card restricts the pool to the combined slate unless every candidate is unfit, and it is a test-owned refactor of the product's own safety net over the running loop's control path -- it is now item (o) and wants its own iteration with a scout lens pointed at it. RIDER, done in the PM stage because it costs the engineer nothing: the compaction 141 owed 142. The binding budget turned out NOT to be the 60,000 `ROADMAP_SIZE_WARN_CHARS` both scouts priced against -- `tests/test_iter140_behavior.py:565` and `tests/test_iter141_behavior.py:461` each assert `len(index) < 54000` against the LIVE file, so real headroom was 749 chars against ~835 chars/iteration of growth: LESS THAN ONE ITERATION, i.e. iteration 143 would have reddened the suite by recording itself. Fixed by archiving the SEVEN-paragraph STATUS stack (3,630 chars) and the stale `RE-MEASURED` DEADLINE blocks (1,193 chars) VERBATIM below, after hoisting every live clause FIRST: item (m)'s ONLY description (archiving that paragraph blind would have DESTROYED the item, which is the trap 141 warned about), and new item (o). Net index 53,251 -> 52,022 chars, headroom 749 -> 1,978. Deliberately stopped there rather than also archiving the SHIPPED (a)/(b)/(e)/(f) prose, because over-moving live prose is what cost 141 a repair. ONE CORRECTION SHIPPED WITH IT: the index carried an instruction from iteration 136 that deleting old `- iter N ` ledger rows is SAFE compaction -- it is WRONG and would redden the suite, because `tests/test_iter122_behavior.py:179` asserts a CLOSED FROZEN SET of 98 iteration numbers each still has a row in the index. - **iter 143 -- `foundry prd-init` renders a schema-valid `prd.json` from an EXPLICIT story list, so the fleet's shipped-but-starved progress meter finally has a PRODUCER (scout B's new-capability lens, candidate B2; advances increment-table item 1 to bite 2c).** The measured problem, re-verified in the PM stage rather than inherited from the slate: roadmap item 1 shipped in two bites -- iter 11 (`prd_status` + `foundry prd`) and iter 12, which wired `dispatch_progress_line` into the dispatcher's shift loop at a LIVE call site (`dispatcher.py`:138) -- and then starved. `cfg.prd` defaults to `<repo>/prd.json` (`foundry.py`:239), NO product config sets `prd`, and all four rostered repos lack the file (checked one by one: agent-foundry, proactive-loop-agent, repolens, resilient-agent-loop-primitives -- all absent; the only `prd*.json` anywhere under `~/projects` belongs to an unrelated kirocrew-study checkout). So that live hook has returned `None` for 143 iterations, `foundry prd` exits 2 for every team, and NOTHING in the tree could produce its input -- no template, no example, the schema documented only inside `prd_status`'s docstring. This is the cheapest available way to make a dead shipped capability live: one producer, ZERO control-path edits. Shape: two pure total functions (`render_prd_doc(titles)` -> `{"stories": [{id: S<n>, title, passes: False}]}` with strip-and-drop normalization; `parse_story_lines(text)` skipping blanks and `#` comments and stripping ONE leading `- `/`* `/`N. ` marker) plus one write-guarded `prd_init_cli` following the `new_product_cli` house convention exactly (0 wrote + self-check clean / 1 wrote but self-check failed / 2 refused and wrote nothing), with all collaborators called by BARE MODULE NAME so `monkeypatch.setattr` bites. THE DESIGN CONSTRAINT THAT IS THE FEATURE: stories come ONLY from explicit sources (repeatable `--story`, plain-text `--from-file`), never from prose. Scout B's own strongest objection was accepted verbatim -- `PLATFORM_ROADMAP.md` is a ~476-line index whose Done ledger alone is ~98 rows, so a scraper yields either ~98 already-done stories or a handful of mis-parses, and a WRONG meter is strictly worse than an absent one because the dispatcher would then log a false figure EVERY shift. Hence no `--from-roadmap` flag, and `passes` is never inferred. Three refusals all precede the write: an EMPTY story set is refused (a 0-story doc would read `0/0 stories pass` forever, and `PrdStatus.complete` is False at `total == 0`, so it is a meter that can never mean anything), an existing target is never clobbered without `--force`, and an unreadable `--from-file` cannot leak an `OSError`. DELIBERATELY NOT DONE, and the reason matters for the next PM: **no `prd.json` is committed for any rostered product, `_platform` included.** For `_platform` `cfg.prd` resolves to `<FOUNDRY>/prd.json`, so landing one would change what the LIVE dispatcher logs mid-run; authoring a real story list is an operator act. The meter therefore stays unfed BY DESIGN and the first honest consumer is probably a product team, not this one. TRIAGE, recorded so it is not re-litigated: scout A's whole slate (narrative-and-docs) was declined on the DIVERSITY GUARD, not on merit -- iteration 142 shipped precisely that shape (a prose fix plus a new dormant docs detector, `bare_foundry_cli_findings`) and all three of A's candidates repeat it. Two of them were ALREADY LISTED here, which the slate did not notice: A's candidate A is item (l) (`ARCHITECTURE.md`:90 + `CONTINUOUS.md`:39 still price the ladder `10->20->40 min`, falsified by 129/135 -- A's re-measurement is nonetheless the sharpest yet: `retry_delay` returns 60/300/1200 for `stalled` and 60/120/240 for `timeout`/`cli-error`, so the doc is right for exactly ONE of three ladders and 10x wrong on the first retry of the other two, and `rg "ARCHITECTURE|CONTINUOUS" tests/test_iter129_behavior.py tests/test_iter135_behavior.py` returns NOTHING, proving a behavior-change iteration's own tests cannot see the docs debt it creates), and A's candidate B is item (m) (the four artifacts disagreeing about five-vs-six invariants). Only A's candidate C was unlisted, so it is now item (p). Scout B's own B1 (stage-level resume; the biggest number on either slate at 8.84 h of already-succeeded stage time discarded across 21 abandoned iteration dirs, mean 25.3 min per event) was declined because bite 1 delivers only a report and bite 2 edits iteration numbering and re-entry -- the semantics the quality bar calls inviolable -- so it needs its own iteration and arguably waits on item (o); B3 (per-attempt JSON evidence sidecar; 458 of 1642 attempt logs, 27.9%, are <=200 bytes of pure failure text) was declined because it edits `run_stage`, the hottest control-path function, for a diagnostic, and it sits next to iteration 126's DE-LISTED partial-output item without closing it -- B3 claims only metadata ABOUT the loss, never the transcript. - **iter 144 -- a read-only `foundry prompt` verb renders the EXACT bytes a stage receives, and a two-sided suite guard proves the digest it shows is the digest `build_prompt` embeds (scout A's new-capability lens, candidate A1).** THE MEASURED PROBLEM, re-verified by the PM stage against the live tree rather than inherited from the slate: the assembled prompt is the largest single input to every stage and nothing in the product can show it. The pm prompt is 18,907 chars and 16,655 of them (88.1%) are the learnings digest, distilled from a 2,928,867-char `LEARNINGS.md` and carrying ONE silent ` [...]` truncation marker; the only surface that exists today reports something else, because `learnings_cli` (`foundry.py`:1553) calls `learnings_digest(text, recent=recent)` WITHOUT the five `PROMPT_LEARNINGS_*` budgets and therefore prints 18,309 chars with ZERO truncation markers -- 1,654 chars off, with the truncation invisible. On top of that `run_stage` (`foundry.py`:13942) sends `prompt + retry_directive(attempt, stage, out_file)`, a further 929-941 chars per seat on attempt 2+ that appear in NO artifact anywhere. Note the scouts measured 16,669/17,861 and TWO markers roughly an hour earlier in the same iteration: the delta is pure log growth, which is precisely the argument for a command instead of a stage transcript, and the reason the spec forbids pinning any of these figures in a test. WHY IT IS WORTH AN ITERATION: the steering-head channel has already cost three (118 head budget, 136 doctor WARN, 138 verbatim-when-it-fits) and every one of them was verifiable only by importing the module in a REPL -- the exact failure the log records being made twice in ONE day while believing steering had landed. SHAPE, all additive and all off the control path: `prompt_stage_options()` / `prompt_stage_args(stage)` derive the five core `(stage, role_file, out_name)` triples from `CORE_SEAT_STAGES` at CALL time so no new stage literal enters the tree; `prompt_learnings_digest(cfg)` applies the five prompt-path budgets, each read as a module global; `render_stage_prompt(cfg, iteration, stage, attempt)` returns byte-for-byte what `run_stage` composes, calling `build_prompt` and `retry_directive` by BARE module name so `monkeypatch.setattr` bites; a frozen `PromptMetrics` + pure `prompt_metrics(prompt, digest, retry)` carry total/digest/retry chars, the floor-percent share, `digest_embedded` and the truncation count; and `prompt_cli` prints `banner + blank line + the verbatim prompt` (so `tail -n +3` is the exact bytes) or a 13-key JSON object, exit 0 rendered / 2 unknown stage. THE DECISION THAT DEFINES THE ITERATION, recorded so it is not re-litigated: `build_prompt` was NOT edited to call the new digest helper. That single-source-of-truth wiring is the PRE-DECLARED bite 2 and needs its own iteration, because `build_prompt` is the hottest function on a running loop's control path. Instead the duplicate computation is made DECIDABLE: behavior 4 asserts the helper's output appears VERBATIM inside `build_prompt`'s return for every core stage, under monkeypatched budgets small enough to force a truncation, so any future drift between the two turns the suite RED and names the duplication instead of silently lying to an operator. Two further invariants are assertions rather than prose: the verb CREATES NOTHING (proved by a before/after byte-and-directory map of the whole tmp tree, not by `assert not target.exists()`, so a `mkdir` of a missing state dir or a littered sibling cannot hide), and the off-control-path claim is a source census with its own non-vacuity floor. - **iter 145 = the roadmap index wall becomes ONE module-level constant, gains a pure headroom verdict, and gets a WARN pedal on `foundry doctor` (scout A's hardening/DX lens, candidate A1).** THE DEFECT, re-measured in the PM stage rather than inherited: the wall that actually fails a ship was a BARE `54000` literal in two tests that read the LIVE file (`tests/test_iter140_behavior.py:565`, `tests/test_iter141_behavior.py:461`), while every runnable tool reported against `ROADMAP_SIZE_WARN_CHARS = 60000` and `roadmap_size_verdict` had ZERO non-test consumers -- so the index measured 52,687 chars with 1,313 chars of real headroom while the only function built to report headroom said 7,313, i.e. 5.6x too generous. Growth over the last 15 index commits, counting only the growing ones, is +987.6 chars/iteration (the negative deltas are the manual archive moves of 139/140/142), so the NEXT growing iteration could redden the live suite by recording itself, and a red suite REVERTS -- the documented cause of the permanent loss of iterations 64 and 122. SHIPPED: `ROADMAP_INDEX_HARD_CHARS = 54000` and `ROADMAP_INDEX_NEAR_WALL_CHARS = 3000` as module globals read AT CALL TIME, a frozen `RoadmapIndexBudget` (`char_count`/`hard_budget`/`near_wall_margin`/`headroom`/`over_budget`/`near_wall`), the pure total `roadmap_index_budget(text)` whose boundary is `>=` so it agrees EXACTLY with the live `len(index) < HARD` brake (a strictly-greater boundary, as `roadmap_size_verdict` uses for its WARN budget, would be off by one and was deliberately NOT copied), a never-raise three-outcome `roadmap_index_line(cfg)` (UNKNOWN/OK/WARN, reading `ProductConfig.roadmap` so it stays repo-agnostic -- verified present in all four live product configs), and ONE new call site: a third drift line in `run_doctor_cli`, exit code UNCHANGED, `run_doctor` still exactly 4 `Check`s (pinned by iter-01, which is why this is a LINE and not a 5th check). The two bare literals now derive from the global, proven two-sided (each assertion still FAILS with the global shrunk below the live size, so the brake is intact rather than hollowed), and the global's VALUE is pinned by a test so a future PM facing a red suite cannot quietly raise the budget instead of archiving. DORMANT on the control path: no call site in `run_iteration`, `run_stage`, `build_prompt` or `dispatcher.py`, no new artifact, no `.gitignore` entry owed, no restart required. The near-wall condition is deliberately ADVISORY and NOT a suite failure -- at 1,313 chars of headroom against a 3,000-char margin it would have reddened the suite on the day it shipped and blocked three iterations of legitimate work. NOT TAKEN: scout B's B1 (`foundry status` surfaces the stage-budget WARN `stage-times` already computes) carried the biggest raw number on either slate -- this product's `engineer` median is AT the 600s hard cap with 11 timeouts in 67 attempts while `status` reports OK -- but it adds another advisory line for a condition already visible via `stage-times`, and it crosses the per-product/fleet-wide boundary (`dispatcher.out` is absent from the fresh clone the post-release gate builds) which its own author calls a mandatory-mitigation risk; it stays the top candidate for the next round. - **iter 146 = the six byte-identical `company_*_cli` bodies collapse into ONE module-level shared body plus six thin wrappers, roadmap item (i) re-scoped from nine members to six (scout B's simplification-and-deletion lens, candidate B1).** PICKED OVER scout A's A3 (give the dormant `dispatch_restart_line` its first producer in `watchdog.py`), which was the highest-value item on either slate and was DISQUALIFIED on a fact verified in the PM stage and named by scout A itself as its disqualifying condition: `crontab -l` reports "no crontab for the current user" and no watchdog agent exists in `~/Library/LaunchAgents`, so `watchdog.py` NEVER FIRES on this machine and A3 would have moved a dormant function into a process that does not run -- answering the integration lens's own question ("what does nothing consult?") with one more unconsulted thing. Scout A's A2 (`live-lag --json`) fell with it, since its only credible consumer was A3; scout A's A1 (a diagnostic invocation in `roles/reviewer.md`) was declined because its only testable claim is that a card CONTAINS a string, so it cannot verify adoption, and it adds a mandatory subprocess to a build stage under the 600s cap; scout B's B3 was excluded by the diversity guard (same file, same two constants, same brakes iteration 145 shipped one commit earlier); scout B's B2 (27 test files) has the highest blast radius on the product's own safety net and its own author ranked it third. **RE-SCOPED BY MEASUREMENT: nine was the wrong family boundary.** The six exact-skeleton members are `company_status_cli` (L8483-8538, 20 docstring lines), `company_weak_tests_cli` (L12007-12063, 21), `company_constant_asserts_cli` (L12290-12347, 22), `company_skipped_tests_cli` (L12591-12654, 28), `company_test_quality_cli` (L12952-13018, 31) and `company_config_lint_cli` (L13264-13325, 26) -- each EXACTLY 36 non-docstring lines, 364 lines total of which 148 are docstring. The other three (`company_history_cli`, `company_timing_cli`, `company_events_cli`) carry extra `limit`/`kind` kwargs and are deliberately left OPEN as the follow-up. So item (i)'s listed "-250 to -350 lines" over nine was billing for docstrings that must survive; the honest figure is -154 non-docstring lines over six. Two members diffed in full vary in EXACTLY four things: the gather seam (once), the summarize seam (twice -- error path and success path), one runtime-erased accumulator annotation (`list[StatusSummary]` vs `list[ConfigLint]`) and two comment words. **THE THREE DESIGN CONSTRAINTS, MEASURED IN THE PM STAGE so no future engineer re-derives them.** (1) The NAMING TRAP from iteration 134's bullet is real and was re-confirmed: the status member's summarize seam is the bare name `summarize_company`, NOT `summarize_company_status`, so any name-derived or stem-built seam dispatch silently misses it -- each wrapper must resolve BOTH of its seams as module globals at CALL time and pass them in as explicit callables, never capturing one at def-time and never taking one as a default argument. (2) The shared body MUST be MODULE-LEVEL, not nested inside a wrapper, and the reason is mechanical: the two live code-object assertions (`tests/test_iter59_behavior.py:951-955`, `tests/test_iter61_behavior.py:715-719`) run `_fn_names_consts` (defined at `tests/test_iter59_behavior.py:189`), which walks `fn.__code__` and recurses ONLY into code objects found in `co_consts` -- i.e. nested functions, lambdas and comprehensions -- and does NOT follow a call to a module-level function. So a module-level body keeps its references invisible to every wrapper, while a NESTED body would leak its names into the enclosing wrapper's `co_names` and could trip the negative cross-CLI assertions at `tests/test_iter59_behavior.py:940-946` and `tests/test_iter61_behavior.py:704-709`. Both positive assertions are `in` checks, so a wrapper that names both seams in order to pass them satisfies them unchanged; both negative assertions survive because the new shared-body name appears in no iteration's `NEW_SYMBOLS` tuple. NEITHER test file may be edited to accommodate the refactor -- if it cannot satisfy them as written the DESIGN is wrong. (3) A `_`-prefixed private name is the right choice and follows convention: `foundry.py` already holds 24 module-level `_`-prefixed defs, so a private body leaves the public surface at 219 defs. **ALSO CHECKED AND CLEAR:** no test calls `inspect.getsource` on any of the six; no test pins `foundry.py`'s line count; `tests/test_iter124_behavior.py` asserts non-empty `__doc__` over a name list, so all 148 per-verb docstring lines must survive on the wrappers. The family is entirely OFF the control path -- nothing in `run_iteration`, `run_stage`, `build_prompt`, `run_continuous` or `dispatcher.py` calls any of the six -- which is what makes this product's FIRST non-additive consolidation of shipped code a safe shape: the 48 `load_config` patches across 9 modules, each verb's own `gather_*` patches, and the six CLI names patched by 8 modules to assert `main()` routing are a loud, immediate oracle, so a patchability regression reddens the suite rather than passing silently. **A METHOD NOTE worth keeping:** the shell's output compression rewrote the long `company_*_cli` identifiers to the single letter `l` in a `grep` result, which made `_fn_names_consts(foundry.company_test_quality_cli)` read as `_fn_names_consts(foundry.l)` and made an introspection assertion look like a trivial one; the census had to be re-run with raw output before it could be believed, exactly as iteration 145's FINAL lesson warned for numeric literals.
- **iter 147 -- BOTH review gates route on the ANCHORED reviewer verdict instead of a raw substring scan.** The gate was `contains(review, "CHANGES_REQUIRED")`, so a reviewer report that merely QUOTED the fail sentinel while explaining itself burned a whole `fix-review` round under the ~600 s cap. The anchored reader `parse_review_verdict` had ALREADY shipped in iter 100 and was simply never wired to the gate -- a brake with no pedal -- and the identical defect on the sibling TEST gate was fixed in iter 127, so this iteration is that proven shape applied one gate over: pure `classify_review_report(text) -> "APPROVE"|"CHANGES_REQUIRED"|"NONE"`, the single I/O seam `read_review_disposition(path)`, the pure predicate `needs_review_repair(disposition)`, and the module-level `REVIEW_GATE_REPAIR_DISPOSITIONS = ("CHANGES_REQUIRED",)`. **MEASURED BY THE ITER-147 PM over all 256 fleet `review*.md` artifacts** (re-derived, not inherited from the scout): the old trigger fires 30 times, the anchored trigger 28, so 2 fires were spurious -- `_platform/iter-146` and `_platform/iter-70`, both ending `VERDICT: APPROVE`, and a `fix_review.md` (6,648 / 3,029 bytes) proves each one really ran a stage. Honest qualification recorded in the spec: iter-146 is the clean proof (0 `[BLOCKING]`, 6 `[NIT]`) while iter-70 carried 1 `[BLOCKING]` under an APPROVE verdict, so that round was mis-routed rather than pure waste. **The trap is SELF-REINFORCING, which is why it recurs: in both cases the token appeared because the reviewer was DESCRIBING THE REVIEW GATE ITSELF**, so any reviewer who reasons about the mechanism trips it. **TWO gates, not one, and finding the second was the iteration's real work:** `run_iteration` (L14107) AND `run_execution_plan` (L3968), the latter WIRED at L14070 behind the manifest guard, so leaving it on the substring scan would have left two live gates disagreeing for a future strangler step to inherit. `"NONE"` is kept OUT of the repair set for the same measured reason iter 127 kept it out (pre-existing routing drivers write an unscripted report as the empty string), which also yields the iteration's headline property: the change is STRICTLY NARROWING -- `needs_review_repair(classify_review_report(t))` implies `"CHANGES_REQUIRED" in t`, so the new gate can NEVER fire where the old one did not, verified with zero violations over all 256 real artifacts. `read_review_disposition` returns `"NONE"` on `OSError` (not `"RED"` like its test-gate sibling) because each degrades to its OWN pre-change route: the `contains()` it replaced returned `False` and ran no fix pass. **The blast radius on existing tests was enumerated mechanically BEFORE the spec was written, and it is exactly three assertions in two files:** `tests/test_iter70_behavior.py` L277/L291 script `reports={"reviewer": "CHANGES_REQUIRED"}` -- a BARE token that now classifies `"NONE"`, so both become `"VERDICT: CHANGES_REQUIRED"`, preserving intent; and `tests/test_iter127_behavior.py` L575's `assert REVIEW_NEEDLE in needles` was a `contains`-spy positive control asserting the REVIEW gate still substring-scans, which is precisely what this iteration removes, so it retargets onto the surviving `RELEASE_NEEDLE` control. Tests that write one uniform multi-sentinel body to EVERY stage file (iter-72 `SHIP_LINES`, iter-127 `SHIP_BODY`) are UNAFFECTED and were checked: the reviewer file's last line is `ACTION: PUSHED ...`, so the verdict is `"NONE"` and no fix pass runs -- the same outcome the old trigger produced by finding no token. `parse_review_verdict` itself is untouched (18 assertions in `tests/test_iter100_behavior.py` already pin it); this iteration WIRES it. Scout A's A1 (the last three extra-kwarg `company_*_cli` wrappers) was the strongest alternative and was REJECTED as a near-clone of iter 146 under the PM card's diversity guard, not on merit -- it remains item (i)'s named follow-up and is unharmed, touching a different region and no shared code. Scout A's A3 (`sentinel_remainder` behind the five sentinel parsers) was declined as scout A itself ranked it: five control-path gate readers for 15-23 lines. **Deliberately NOT bundled:** relaxing `roles/reviewer.md`'s prose discipline, which this fix makes safe, is item (d)'s shape and carries item (d)'s own constraint -- it must not ship in the same iteration as the gate fix that makes it safe, because a live loop holds an older `foundry.py` in memory.
- **iter 148 -- `stage-times` attributes every no-output stage attempt to a failure KIND.** Scout A candidate A1, BITE 1 of 2. The defect was re-measured by this PM rather than inherited (`products/_platform/state/iter-148/probe_pm148_kinds.py`): `dispatcher.out` holds 80 `no output file` lines, `summarize_stage_times` reports exactly 80 across its groups under a field literally named `timeouts` (`foundry.py:11229`, `timeouts=sum(1 for a in items if not a.produced)`), and only 40 of those 80 carry a cap-timeout marker -- a MISLABEL FACTOR of exactly 2.00x. Measured kind mix over the same 80: `timeout` 40, `stalled` 24, `service` 13, `other` 2, `cli-error` 1. Five positive controls were asserted in ONE block so a dead matcher could not report a clean zero: the corpus has failures at all, the field equals the not-produced count, at least 3 distinct kinds appear, the `timeout` marker is not dead, and the field is STRICTLY greater than the genuine timeout count (without that last one the premise is vacuous). The value is not the corrected label but the per-stage split it exposes: `engineer` is `{timeout: 19, cli-error: 1}` with ZERO stalls while `tester` is `{stalled: 19, timeout: 7, service: 2, other: 2}` -- opposite remedies (smaller engineer bites vs. no local command silent for 120s), and today one column reports 20 and 30 with no way to tell them apart. The framework has ACTED on kind since iters 129/135 (`KIND_RETRY_LADDERS`) but reported nothing about it, so a ladder change could not be evaluated. Small because both halves already ship: `classify_attempt_failure` is a pure, total, case-insensitive classifier over exactly these markers, every real no-output line already carries `; tail: '<blob>'`, and `parse_stage_attempts` merely discarded that tail. Shape: one TRAILING `kind: str = ""` on `StageAttempt`, one TRAILING `kind_counts: tuple[tuple[str, int], ...] = ()` on `StageTimesGroup` (ascending by kind, zeros excluded, summing to `timeouts`), a `kinds ` fragment appended to the render for failing groups only, and two `to_dict` keys. Compatibility was CHECKED BEFORE speccing, not assumed: `tests/test_iter117_behavior.py` asserts group keys with `set(g) >= {...}` (a SUPERSET test) and its render checks are substring `any(...)` checks, no test anywhere constructs `StageAttempt` directly, and its iter-117 behavior-10 dormancy tests pin `parse_stage_attempts`/`summarize_stage_times` off the control path -- all still hold under an additive trailing field. The existing `timeouts` field KEEPS its exact meaning (all no-output attempts) because 26 shipped iterations are not live in the running brain and a dormant consumer may read it; `kind_counts` is what makes the number honest. DEFERRED to bite 2: pricing the dispatcher SLEEP between a failed attempt and its retry (scout A measured 19.50 h, 2.0x the failed agent time), which needs a second pending-event and a new timestamp pairing -- split out because `_platform`'s engineer stage median already sits AT the 600s cap. STRONGEST ALTERNATIVE REJECTED: scout B's B1, the `10->20->40 min` retry ladder that `ARCHITECTURE.md`:90 and `CONTINUOUS.md`:39 still print and that B1 measured as misdescribing 130 of 151 real retries. B1 is smaller and safer and is a genuine defect; it lost only because it corrects prose a human reads occasionally while A1 corrects a number the product itself REPORTS, and because A1 adds the per-kind split that decides which reliability fix a team needs next. A1 also makes B1 cheaper: once the report prints the kind mix, B1's corrected figures come off a CLI instead of a scout probe. B1 is untouched and remains the recommended next docs pick (roadmap item (l)). Scout B's B3 (roadmap item (m), the prompt-inlined `quality_bar` naming two invariants with 0 hits in `ARCHITECTURE.md`) is the one candidate with LIVE blast radius -- role cards and `config.json` are read from disk at stage time -- so it wants a reviewer reading it as a prompt change, not as a doc, and it stays open. Scout A's own biggest number is NOT on its slate and is recorded here for the next PM: all 71 retry sleeps still show a 600s median, i.e. the OLD uniform ladder, because iters 129/135 are among the 26 shipped-but-not-live iterations (`doctor`: live-lag WARN, brain launched 08-05 15:13:46). Re-pricing the 35 cap-timeout sleeps from 600s to 60s alone accounts for roughly 9.6 h of the 19.50 h, so the largest throughput win available is an ACTIVATION, gated on roadmap item (o) -- which is TEST-OWNED, has the highest blast radius on the list, and must get its own iteration.
- **iter 149 -- the prompt-inlined `quality_bar` stops citing two design invariants that do not exist, and a pure `quality_bar_invariant_gaps` brake keeps the citation honest (scout A's narrative-and-docs lens, candidate A2; roadmap item (m), now CLOSED).** THE DEFECT, re-measured in this PM stage rather than inherited from the slate: `build_prompt` inlines `quality_bar` verbatim into EVERY stage prompt (`- Product quality bar: ...`), so all ~9-11 agent stages of every `_platform` iteration were told to "preserve the design invariants documented in ARCHITECTURE.md (output-file success, anti-delegation, revert-on-doubt gate, tester isolation, single-brain dispatch)" -- and case-insensitively against the file it names, `revert-on-doubt` has **0 hits** and `single-brain dispatch` has **0 hits** (`single-brain` alone has 3, but as a section-4 RULE, not a section-3 invariant), while the three that do resolve (`output-file success`, `anti-delegation`, `tester isolation`) have 1 hit each. The real section-3 invariant **Resilience** (`ARCHITECTURE.md:90`) was OMITTED, and the actual name of the thing "revert-on-doubt gate" gestures at is **Independent, pessimistic gate** (`ARCHITECTURE.md:71`). So two of five names sent every agent to a file where they cannot be found -- the prompt was training agents to distrust their own quality bar. DIRECTION OF THE FIX IS FORCED: `VISION.md` ratifies the document's names, so the DOC WINS and this is config-VALUE-only; renaming the invariants in `ARCHITECTURE.md` to match the bar would be the same edit in the wrong direction. SHIPPED: two pure, total, never-raising functions (`quality_bar_invariants(bar) -> tuple[str, ...]` and `quality_bar_invariant_gaps(bar, doc) -> tuple[str, ...]`, no dataclass, no CLI verb, no filesystem, no call site in `run_iteration`/`run_stage`/`build_prompt`/`dispatcher.py`) plus ONE JSON string value -- no schema change and no new key, so iteration 128's unknown-key guard and `lint-config` are untouched. The replacement cites `output-file success, anti-delegation, pessimistic gate, tester isolation, resilience, single-brain`, all six findable verbatim, and is 432 chars against 433 before, so the prompt char budget moved by -1. THREE MEASURED HAZARDS recorded so no future engineer re-derives them. (1) The citation ANCHOR is mandatory: the bar contains an earlier parenthesized group, `importable (python -c import)`, so a naive "first `(...)` group" parse returns `("python -c import",)` and the guard becomes nonsense -- the extraction must anchor on the citation phrase. (2) Comma is the separator, so cited names must be COMMA-FREE, which is exactly why the bar cites `pessimistic gate` rather than the doc's full heading `Independent, pessimistic gate`. Matching is substring under case-folding plus whitespace collapse -- deliberately NO stemming and NO stripping of trailing words like `gate`, because that kindness is what would make the brake vacuous. (3) The VACUITY TRAP the scout named and the spec closes: a future re-wording that breaks extraction would make the gap assertion silently pass over an empty name list, so behavior 8 asserts the live extraction is NON-EMPTY (`>= 5` names) and behavior 10 is a two-sided control -- substituting one cited name for `nonexistent-invariant` against the real `ARCHITECTURE.md` must return exactly `("nonexistent-invariant",)`. Both halves were PROVEN ACHIEVABLE in the PM stage with a throwaway prototype before the spec was handed off: the CURRENT bar yields exactly `("revert-on-doubt gate", "single-brain dispatch")`, the REPLACEMENT yields `()`, and the no-citation and unbalanced-paren inputs yield `()`. STRONGEST ALTERNATIVE REJECTED: scout B's B2 (`foundry retry-advice`, classifying why an iteration did not ship) carried the biggest number on either slate -- 18 of 25 decided non-ships never wrote `final.md` at all, i.e. ~72% of non-ships are environmental and re-doable -- and it lost on the PM card's DIVERSITY GUARD rather than on merit: a pure classifier plus frozen dataclass plus read-only analysis CLI over an on-disk corpus is the same shape as iteration 148 (`stage-times` splitting attempts by failure kind), one commit earlier. B2 is untouched and remains the top new-capability pick; whoever takes it must handle its author's own hardest-graded hazard, `is_latest`, because the newest iteration always lacks `final.md` for the obvious reason and a classifier that calls that a stage-kill libels its most-read row. Scout A's A1 (roadmap item (l): `ARCHITECTURE.md`:90 and `CONTINUOUS.md`:39 still price the retry ladder `10->20->40 min`, falsified by iterations 129/135) stays the recommended next docs pick and is now CHEAPER, since iteration 148 made the live kind mix readable off a CLI; its unsolved half is still guard design, because the corrected figures reduce to the small integers 1/2/4/5/10/20/40/60 that already occur throughout a 243-line doc. Scout A's A3 (roadmap item (p): `new-product` is absent from `README.md` ENTIRELY, not merely from the `# N.` index) also stays open, with its measured tripwire -- `tests/test_iter117_behavior.py:472` pins the literal `# 42.`, so the new entry must be APPENDED as the next free number rather than inserted in logical position. RIDER, done in this PM stage: both scouts independently measured the index at 50,289 chars against the `ROADMAP_INDEX_HARD_CHARS` wall of 54,000 enforced live by `tests/test_iter140_behavior.py::test_b10_index_is_under_the_declared_budget`, i.e. 3,711 chars of headroom at ~988 chars/iteration and only ~711 chars above the advisory 3,000-char `ROADMAP_INDEX_NEAR_WALL_CHARS` WARN band -- about ONE iteration from the WARN, not four. Retiring item (m)'s spent prose in the same commit that ships it more than paid for this iteration's own ledger row, so the index NET SHRANK 50,289 -> 50,262 chars (-27) instead of growing, and the stale `STILL OPEN` enumeration was corrected from `(j)-(m)` to `(j)-(l)`. Reclaiming real headroom by archiving spent prose was deliberately kept OUT of this iteration's feature scope -- it is a docs-policed edit that does not belong stacked on a config change -- and is named the top scheduling item for iter 150.
- **iter 151 -- `revert_repo` captures the uncommitted tree to `ABORTED_IMPLEMENTATION.patch` before its hard reset, via the new total seam `capture_abort_patch` (scout A's hardening/DX lens, candidate A1).** THE LOSS, re-measured in this PM stage rather than inherited: `revert_repo` (`foundry.py:539`) is three lines -- `git reset --hard origin/<branch>`, `git clean -fd`, `log(...)` -- reached from exactly **13 call sites** (plus 4 doc/comment mentions), and it captures NOTHING: `capture_abort_patch`, `ABORTED_IMPLEMENTATION` and `REVERTED_IMPLEMENTATION` each occur **0 times** in `foundry.py`, counted with `str.count` because this shell's `grep` display rewrote the identifier `revert_repo` to `ln` and to `c` in two separate results. **Iteration 150 is the fresh casualty and it is unrecoverable:** four green stages on disk (`engineer.md` 9,234B, `reviewer.md` 12,421B, `fix_review.md` 7,911B), no `tester.md`, no `final.md`, and four 166-byte attempt logs all reading `agent run failed: subagent turn failed: Connection stalled - no data received for 120 s`. Because the work was never committed, `git reflog` shows the reset landing directly on iter 149's commit with nothing between and `git stash list` is empty, so there is no dangling object and no `fsck --lost-found` rescue. Scout A's fleet scan puts the class at **73 stall attempts across 30 iterations, 9 of which died before the gate** (8 of the 9 are this team), corroborated independently by `stage-times`: of 29 attempts classified `stalled`, **26 are in a test-RUNNING stage**. **REVEALED PREFERENCE settles the artifact's value and no argument is needed:** iterations 120/121/125 carry HAND-SAVED `REVERTED_IMPLEMENTATION.patch` files (40,961 / 51,824 / 47,619 bytes) that `foundry.py` never wrote, and the roadmap's iteration-121 RETRY item exists ONLY because a human happened to save 51,824 bytes by hand. Iteration 150 got no hand-save. **WHAT THIS DOES NOT CLAIM:** it does not stop the stall -- the 120s no-data watchdog belongs to the agent CLI, not to us -- it downgrades the stall's cost from unrecoverable to a verbatim retry, which is a far cheaper problem than owning the watchdog. **THE BLAST RADIUS IS ZERO, AND THAT IS MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED:** twelve test files mention `revert_repo` and every single mention is a `monkeypatch.setattr(foundry, "revert_repo", ...)` seam replacement -- `foundry.revert_repo(` occurs **0 times** across all 133 files in `tests/`, so no existing test executes the body being changed. **THREE DESIGN CONSTRAINTS FIXED IN THE SPEC so no engineer re-derives them.** (1) The guard belongs at the CALL SITE, not inside the capture: `try: capture_abort_patch(cfg, reason) / except Exception: pass` in `revert_repo`, mirroring the existing `emit_event` guard inside `log()`. An inner-only guard passes a happy-path test but a monkeypatched-to-raise seam would still stop the reset, and `revert_repo` is the loop's safety mechanism -- a revert that cannot run leaves a dirty tree that poisons the NEXT iteration, which is strictly worse than a lost patch. That is why behavior 3 (raise the capture, assert `reset --hard` then `clean -fd` still issue in order and `log` still fires) is named the BLOCKING acceptance criterion, ahead of any assertion about patch CONTENT. (2) The iteration number is DERIVED, not threaded: `revert_repo(cfg, reason)` never receives it and passing it would touch all 13 call sites, so the capture selects the highest-numbered existing `iter-NN` under `cfg.state` -- correct during a live abort because every prior stage has already written into that dir -- reusing the pure total `iteration_numbers` (`foundry.py:8720`) and selecting the actual `Path` rather than rebuilding an `iter-{n:02d}` name. No `iter-*` dir means write nothing and return `None`. (3) `git add -A -N` runs BEFORE `git diff HEAD` because the most valuable lost bytes are UNTRACKED (the new `tests/test_iterNN_behavior.py`) and `git clean -fd` is about to delete exactly those; `-N` records intent-to-add and stages no content, and the only caller resets the index on its very next statement, which is precisely why it is safe -- so the coupling is a docstring requirement and `capture_abort_patch` must gain no second call site. **DELIBERATELY OUT:** a synthesized header/timestamp/reason banner inside the patch (the file stays PURE diff bytes so `git apply` takes it unmodified, and the reason already reaches `NIGHT_LOG.md` via `revert_repo`'s own log line); auto-APPLYING a patch or any verb to list/replay them; purifying the `git` seam, which returns `(p.stdout + p.stderr).strip()` so a git warning on stderr can land in the patch text -- pre-existing for every caller and a separate iteration. **NO `.gitignore` BITE IS OWED, and that is checked rather than argued:** `git check-ignore -v` reports `.gitignore:2:products/*/state/` for the patch path, so it is already ignored; for non-`_platform` products the patch lands in the foundry checkout's ignored state dir and never in the product repo, so it cannot leak in either direction. **STRONGEST ALTERNATIVE REJECTED:** scout B's B2, one `dispatcher_proc_match` liveness probe replacing three divergent `pgrep` patterns, which carried the best measurements on either slate -- run live against the process table, the broad `dispatcher.py` pattern returned 4 pids of which **2 are false positives** (an operator shell script and the agent-run process itself, both merely carrying the string in argv), so `watchdog.py` can refuse the resurrection it exists to perform, while the launcher's narrow hard-coded pattern returned 1 pid and MISSED the real dispatcher, whose argv begins `/opt/homebrew/.../MacOS/Python` (capital P, and `pgrep -f` is case-sensitive), so an operator running `./launch_dispatcher.sh` after a watchdog resurrection is told nothing is running and starts a SECOND BRAIN. It lost on BITE STRUCTURE, not merit: bite 1 changes only what two read-only verbs PRINT, the payoff needs bite 2 (`launch_dispatcher.sh` consulting `preflight`, `watchdog.py` dropping its private `_pgrep`), and B2's own author names 'bite 1 ships, bite 2 never lands' as the fate of `gate-scope` and the iteration-141 restart flag. A1 is complete in one bite. B2 bite 1 remains the top new pick and its two-sided fixture already exists as the four REAL argv strings in `pm_scout_b.md` measurement 4. **ALSO OPEN, unharmed:** scout A's A2 (one total `read_text_or_none` guarding `(OSError, ValueError)` over the **6 control-path** narrow read sites -- `build_prompt` reading `LEARNINGS.md` is the worst, since a partial multibyte append would raise for every stage of every product; iteration 150 fixed exactly one READ-ONLY site) and scout B's B1 / roadmap item (p) (`new-product` is the 1 of 46 verbs with 0 `README.md` mentions, tripwire already measured: `tests/test_iter117_behavior.py:472` pins the literal `# 42.`, so a new entry must be APPENDED as the next free number). Scout A's A3 was declined by its own author's ranking -- it prices the 120s watchdog we do not own, and its most useful number is recorded in the spec instead: one serial suite run is now **57.70s, 48% of the watchdog**, up from 46.58s at iter 136 (+24% in ~14 iterations), so any single command chaining two suite runs (115.4s) is an automatic kill TODAY. **RIDER, done in this PM stage:** the Done-ledger row was re-cut from 121 to exactly 120 chars to honour the index contract's own limit (a mechanical check over every `- iter` row now reports 0 rows over 120), and the index measures 50,383 chars against the `ROADMAP_INDEX_HARD_CHARS` wall of 54,000, i.e. **3,617 chars of headroom** -- 617 above the advisory 3,000-char `ROADMAP_INDEX_NEAR_WALL_CHARS` band, so reclaiming headroom by archiving spent prose (named for iter 150, which never shipped) is now roughly ONE growing iteration away and is the top scheduling item for iter 152.**
- **iter 152 -- the three extra-kwarg `company_*_cli` stragglers fold into `_company_rollup_cli`, closing roadmap item (i) at nine of nine (scout B's simplification-and-deletion lens, candidate B1).** THE VARIATION IS EXACTLY THREE MECHANICAL THINGS, measured in this PM stage against the shipped shared body rather than inherited: (1) the `gather` seam takes extra POSITIONALS -- `gather_history(cfg, limit)` (`foundry.py:8986`), `gather_timing(cfg, limit)` (`:11034`), `gather_events(cfg, kind, limit)` (`:13709`); (2) the `summarize` seam takes extra KWARGS -- nothing for history (`summarize_company_history` `:10723`), `threshold=` for timing (`:12069`), `kind_filter=kind` for events (`:14012`), and each extra kwarg must be passed on BOTH the synthetic-error path and the normal path, which is where a hand-collapse loses it; (3) the accumulator ANNOTATION (`list[HistorySummary]` / `list[TimingSummary]` / `list[EventsSummary]`) differs from the shared body's `list[object]` -- annotation only, no runtime effect, so it is deliberately surrendered. THE ONE TRAP, and it is a real one: `company_timing_cli` reads `threshold = SUITE_SLOW_SECONDS` INSIDE its body (`foundry.py:12118`, with the comment `read at call time -- monkeypatch bites`), so hoisting it into a DEFAULT ARGUMENT of either the wrapper or the shared body freezes it at import and silently kills `monkeypatch.setattr(foundry, "SUITE_SLOW_SECONDS", ...)`. The wrapper keeps that assignment as its first statement and passes the value in the kwargs mapping. BLAST RADIUS MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED: `_company_rollup_cli` occurs **0 times across all 133 files in `tests/`**, so no test pins the shared body's signature and adding two optional parameters cannot break one; each straggler is pinned by exactly ONE test file -- `tests/test_iter31_behavior.py` (history, 9 refs), `tests/test_iter40_behavior.py` (timing, 10), `tests/test_iter46_behavior.py` (events, 9) -- and all three are behavior tests on the PUBLIC names; `tests/test_iter146_behavior.py` states in its own header that no `inspect.getsource` is used anywhere in it, so nothing in the suite asserts the body TEXT of any family member. THE PRECEDENT SHAPE IS ALREADY IN THE FILE: `company_status_cli` is a two-line body -- `return _company_rollup_cli(dispatch_path, as_json, gather_status, summarize_company)` -- under the comment `Seams resolved HERE, by BARE name at CALL time, so a monkeypatch bites`. The three new wrappers copy it exactly, adding a positional tuple and a kwargs mapping. Note the `summarize_company` naming trap iteration 134 recorded: status's summarize is NOT `summarize_company_status`, so no name-derived dispatch may be introduced -- every seam is passed explicitly. SIZE HONESTY: the three bodies are 58 / 63 / 62 lines, but 9-line docstrings survive a collapse, so the saving is body-lines minus wrapper-lines, est. -130 to -145 in `foundry.py`, not the raw 183 the text-diff suggests. Code-level similarity to the shared body (AST-unparsed, docstrings stripped) was 0.970 / 0.940 / 0.953 -- raw-text `difflib` reads only 91.3-91.6% because docstrings are the bulk of these functions and a text ratio measures prose.
