Roadmap index headroom at the start of this iteration was 49,870 of `ROADMAP_INDEX_HARD_CHARS` 54,000, growing ~988 chars/iteration, so a compaction is due within about four iterations and this iteration kept its own index addition small. Successor listed as item (q).
- **iter 154 -- `test_iter61_behavior.py`'s read-only-CLI tree guard compares GIT-VISIBLE state instead of byte-walking the real `products/` tree, killing a live false-RED hazard and the suite's 3rd slowest test (scout A's performance-and-throughput lens, candidate A1).** THE GUARD'S INTENT SURVIVES; ITS MECHANISM WAS THE DEFECT. `_snapshot_tree(_ROOT / "products")` (`tests/test_iter61_behavior.py:214`, called at `:765` and `:768`) built a path->`read_bytes()` dict for EVERY file under `products/` -- measured this iteration at 6574 files / 50.8 MB, read TWICE -- so the guard was (a) 3.77 s in-suite / 5.72 s standalone, the 3rd slowest test in a suite that has grown 35.41 s (iter 126) -> 46.58 s (iter 136) -> 55.07 s now, and (b) GUARANTEED to fail whenever anything wrote under `products/` while it ran. THE HAZARD IS LIVE, NOT HYPOTHETICAL, AND IT FIRED IN THIS VERY ITERATION: scout A's own `-n auto` measurement redirected suite output to `products/_platform/state/iter-154/suite_parallel.txt`, pytest's diff named that exact path, and scout A then settled it two-sidedly in 10 s -- the test PASSES serially with no concurrent writer and FAILS serially with a trivial background appender under `products/`, so parallelism was never the cause. That matters because every runtime artifact this loop writes lives under `products/` (`products/*/state/`, `events.jsonl`, `NIGHT_LOG.md`, `LEARNINGS.md`, `STATUS_REPORT.md`), and redirecting a command's output into the state dir is ENCOURAGED stage behavior -- so a false RED was reachable by an ordinary, well-behaved stage. Its cost is asymmetric and worse than slowness: at the tester stage a false RED writes `RESULT: FAIL`, an unconditional ship blocker per the standing operator directive; at the final gate it reverts verified work, which is exactly the class of loss iterations 150 and 153's FINAL lessons are about. GIT ALREADY DRAWS THE RIGHT LINE AND IT IS 370x CHEAPER, MEASURED IN THIS PM STAGE: everything the loop writes under `products/` is gitignored (`.gitignore` lines 2-16), only **4** files under `products/` are TRACKED, and `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all -- products/` plus `git ls-files -s -- products/` together cost 0.01 s against the walk's ~1.9 s. So the narrowed guard still catches every class a ship diff can carry while ignoring exactly the runtime state no ship diff ever carries. THE DESIGN WAS PROTOTYPED AND PROVEN BEFORE IT WAS SPECCED, not handed over as a hypothesis: a two-layer snapshot (sorted porcelain lines + sorted `ls-files -s` index lines) was run against a hermetic `git init` repo under a temp dir and reported UNEQUAL for all three known-positives -- a new not-ignored file, an in-place append to a committed tracked file, and a `git add`-ed new file -- and EQUAL for the known-negative, a write under a committed-`.gitignore` `state/` path. THE DECISIVE CONTROL RAN AGAINST THE REAL TREE: with a background thread appending **20,688,896 bytes** to a file under `products/_platform/state/iter-154/` throughout, before and after snapshots of the real `products/` compared EQUAL, i.e. the precise failure mode is eliminated, not merely made less likely. Bounds also measured: the snapshot's `repr()` is **335 chars** (against the 20,000 the spec allows and the >40,000,000 the byte-walk yielded) and 20 consecutive real-tree snapshots take **0.46 s**. WHY THE NARROWING IS NOT FAIL-OPEN, which is the strongest objection scout A raised against its own candidate: the invariant that matters is 'a read-only verb leaves nothing a ship diff would carry', and the two layers cover new not-ignored files, in-place edits of tracked files, deletions AND index mutations. What it deliberately stops catching is a write into a gitignored runtime path -- which no ship diff contains and which the loop itself performs by design. The spec therefore makes the three known-positives BLOCKING behaviors in the same new test file, so the net cannot quietly become decorative. BLAST RADIUS IS EXACTLY ONE TEST, MEASURED NOT ASSUMED: `_snapshot_tree` is defined independently in 5+ test files (iters 17/21/23/48/59/43/54/57/61) and `test_iter61_behavior.py:765` is the ONLY call anywhere that passes a REAL repo directory -- every other caller passes pytest's `tmp_path`, where a byte-walk is small, hermetic and correct. `_snapshot_tree` has exactly one consumer in iter 61, so it is deleted with its last call site. SCOUT A's ONE EXTRAPOLATION WAS FALSIFIED IN THIS STAGE AND IS NOW DE-LISTED AS ITEM (r): its slate proposed applying the same fix to the four `live_smoke_on_real_dispatch_config` tests (23.02 s combined) and estimated the suite would land near the iter-126 ~35 s figure. It would not -- all four snapshot `tmp_path`, so they contain no real-tree walk to fix, and iteration 153 had ALREADY measured their true cost as real per-verb AST scanning of every product's tests (7.03/4.56/2.67/2.15/0.18 s across five DIFFERENT verbs, with no shared setup to cache). Recording the falsification is the point: the extrapolation was plausible, cheap to check, and would have cost a future iteration a whole shift. TRIAGED AGAINST scout B's A1, the strongest alternative and a genuinely close call: `ARCHITECTURE.md:90` and `CONTINUOUS.md:39` still price `BACKOFFS` 10->20->40 min as the only retry ladder when `retry_delay` has consulted `KIND_RETRY_LADDERS["stalled"]` (60/300/1200) and `TIMEOUT_BACKOFFS` (60/120/240) first since iters 129/135, and scout B pre-verified the oracle fleet-wide -- of 90 real retries across 1,201 attempts, 75 (83%) drew a ladder neither doc mentions. It lost on CONSEQUENCE, not on evidence quality: a wrong sentence misleads a human who reads it occasionally, while this guard can destroy a shift for a reason unrelated to the change, and it had already misfired once today. Item (l) is untouched and remains cheap. ALSO OPEN, unharmed: scout A's B1 (the parallel `-n auto` build-stage command, item (k), re-measured today at 55.07 s serial vs 20.97 s / 2.63x with 4757 of 4758 verdicts identical INCLUDING all 26 every-suite freeze guards -- which retires the 'many workers git-diffing one tree' objection outright, and this iteration removes the single verdict that DID change, so (k) is strictly safer after 154 than before it) and scout A's C1 (the pinned learnings head at 9,248 of its 10,000-char budget, 92.5% full, with the spent 2026-08-04 fixes (1) and (2) still occupying it). RIDER: the index grew 50,421 -> 50,871 chars, leaving **3,129** of headroom against `ROADMAP_INDEX_HARD_CHARS` 54,000 -- deliberately kept just clear of the 3,000-char `ROADMAP_INDEX_NEAR_WALL_CHARS` advisory band that iter 153 forecast would trip this iteration, so `doctor`'s `roadmap-index:` line stays quiet; a compaction is still due within about three iterations and remains a scheduling item, never a competitive candidate.**
- **iter 155 -- HOTFIX: iteration 154's bounded-snapshot oracle rebuilds its non-vacuity precondition on a hermetic `tmp_path` fixture instead of the ambient `products/` tree (no candidate contest -- duty 0).** `products/_platform/HOTFIX_NEEDED.md` was live against HEAD (`18d28ee44`), so the role card's duty 0 made the hotfix the only permitted feature and both scout slates were recorded rather than triaged. THE DEFECT: iter 154 shipped GREEN here and turned post-release BROKEN because `test_b2_snapshot_is_bounded_not_a_byte_walk` guarded its bounded-ness claim with `assert n_files > 6000` over `_ROOT / "products"` -- 6584 files in this working tree, but only **4** are TRACKED (`products/.gitignore` plus three product `config.json`/`staffing.json`), so the fresh clone the verifier builds fails with `assert 4 > 6000`. Exactly the trap the standing 2026-08-11 operator directive names: a test that passes only on this machine is not a test. **THE FAILURE SET WAS RE-MEASURED IN THE PM STAGE AND THE MEASUREMENT CORRECTED THE SCOPE, WHICH IS THE MOST TRANSFERABLE THING HERE.** A first reproduction used `git clone --depth 1` and reported TWO failures, the second being `tests/test_iter130_behavior.py::test_b14_real_tree_smoke_git_ship_commits`. That one is a PHANTOM OF THE REPRODUCTION METHOD: `verify_fresh_clone` issues `git clone <url> <dir>` with NO `--depth`, while `git_ship_commits` needs history back to iteration 120, which a shallow clone truncates. Against a FULL clone that file is 73/73 green and the whole suite reports **1 failed, 4756 passed, 13 skipped in 41.30s** -- one test function, not two. Had the phantom been believed, the spec would have sent an engineer to 'fix' a healthy iter-130 test on the loop's own failure path. RULE: reproduce a post-release failure with the SAME clone semantics the verifier uses, or you manufacture failures and over-scope the hotfix. **THE REPLACEMENT PROVES A STRICTLY STRONGER CLAIM, prototyped in the PM stage rather than handed over as a hypothesis.** `_git_visible_snapshot` (`tests/test_iter61_behavior.py:214`) is two `git` calls scoped by `-- <root>` (`status --porcelain --untracked-files=all` and `ls-files -s`, sorted), so it is bounded by the GIT-VISIBLE set and never by files on disk -- gitignored bulk is simply not reported. Building two throwaway repos with the file's existing `_hermetic_repo` helper and filling the committed-ignored `state/` path, `repr()` measured **156 chars at 1000 ignored files and 156 chars at 3000** -- byte-identical, because the helper commits identical tracked content in both. So the spec's behavior 6 asserts the two lengths are EQUAL rather than merely under the 20000-char ceiling: a 60x change in on-disk count moving the snapshot by ZERO characters is the real bounded-ness claim, and it is provable anywhere. Costs, measured not estimated: the 3000-file fixture builds in 0.51s, the 1000-file one in 0.24s, a snapshot call takes 0.026s, so ~0.7s is added to a 41.3s fresh-clone suite while the removed `rglob` over 6584 real files was itself costing ~0.2s. **THE HAZARD THE SPEC PINS:** behaviors 5-7 are all 'small or equal' shapes, the one family that passes when the helper is BROKEN (git off PATH, wrong pathspec, an ambient `core.excludesFile`) -- iter 154's own tester lesson. The file's existing `test_nonvacuity_two_distinct_repos_do_not_snapshot_alike` is what keeps them informative, so behavior 9 forbids weakening it, and behavior 4 makes the fill's invisibility to git a `git check-ignore -q` assertion rather than an assumption. Behavior 8 adds a direct fresh-clone SIMULATION: monkeypatch the module global `_REAL_PRODUCTS` to a 4-file directory and CALL the test function, which is the established in-repo pattern (iter 132). **FLAG LIFECYCLE, checked in code so no one hand-edits it:** `HOTFIX_NEEDED.md` is gitignored and `verify_fresh_clone` calls `clear_hotfix_flag` itself on a HEALTHY result (`foundry.py:8167`), so the spec forbids deleting or committing it. **DELIBERATELY DEFERRED, now item (s):** the repo-wide guard banning ambient-tree COUNT preconditions across `tests/` is the right fix for the CLASS, but duty 0 restricts this iteration and the matcher owes a two-sided calibration -- only 2 test files define `_REAL_PRODUCTS` (this one and `test_iter137`, whose uses are `.exists()` leak checks that are fresh-clone-safe), while many legitimate `tmp_path` byte-walks must NOT be flagged. **SCOUT SLATES RECORDED, NOT TRIAGED.** Scout B falsified the MECHANISM of standing operator fix #3 of the 2026-08-04 set: 472 of 1932 attempt logs (24.4%) are byte-identical 48-byte stubs, but only **1** was written by the parent's `except TimeoutExpired` branch, so `capture_output=True` buffering is not the loss -- the child exits normally against its own 600s cap and the buffer arrives intact and empty of transcript. Streaming stdout would fix nothing; a later PM should re-scope or retire that directive. Scout B also measured 40 of 46 verbs already offering `--json` with `novelty-check` returning RUT on the live config, the standing argument against another read-only report. Scout A pre-verified its docs rule two-sidedly and found `new-product` is the 1 verb of 46 with no `README.md` example (item (p), unchanged; `tests/test_iter117_behavior.py:472` pins the literal `# 42.`, so a new entry must be APPENDED as the next free number). **RIDER -- the index SHRANK while gaining two records.** Iter 154 left 3,129 chars of headroom against `ROADMAP_INDEX_HARD_CHARS` (54,000), i.e. inside the 3,000-char advisory band within one iteration, and crossing that wall turns the suite RED and reverts an iteration for a docs-only reason. Three SHIPPED clauses whose detail already lives here were compacted -- (e) 794 -> 275, (b) 445 -> 188, (h) 550 -> 278, reclaiming 1,048 chars -- against the +120 ledger row and the ~600-char item (s), so the index went **50,871 -> 50,462 (-409)** and headroom ROSE to **3,538**, clear of the band. Each clause kept its `(X) SHIPPED iter NNN -- ` prefix because `tests/test_iter133_behavior.py` pins the literal string `(f) SHIPPED iter 133`; no `- iter N ` ledger row was touched (a closed frozen set of 98 numbers is pinned at `tests/test_iter122_behavior.py:179`). **TOOLING HAZARD HIT LIVE, worth the line:** this shell's `grep` DISPLAY rewrote identifiers in its own output -- `rglob` rendered as `n` and `_REAL_PRODUCTS` as `ln` -- and separately `grep`-with-`\|` alternation matched nothing because the built-in is ripgrep. Every count in this record was taken with Python `str.count` / `pathlib` instead, which is the only trustworthy way to census this repo.**
- **iter 156 -- `foundry preship` re-verifies the ship commit from a clone of the repo's OWN LOCAL git tree, in the commit-to-push window, so the verifier's environment is reachable BEFORE the commit is published (scout B's hardening/DX lens, candidate B1).** THE GAP IS STRUCTURAL, NOT ACCIDENTAL, and it was re-measured in this PM stage rather than inherited: `foundry.py` contains exactly ONE `git clone` (line 7961, inside `verify_fresh_clone`) and it clones the value of `git remote get-url origin` -- the REMOTE -- so the cloned tree can only exist AFTER the push. Its sole call site is `postrelease_step`, whose own docstring states it "runs ONLY on the SHIPPED branch (after the push already happened), so a BROKEN verdict never reverts", and `preflight` is doctor + single-brain at LAUNCH time and never clones. THAT IS EXACTLY HOW ITERATION 154 SHIPPED BROKEN: three stages independently reported a green WORKING TREE, and the working tree was never the question -- git tracks only 4 files under `products/`, so an assertion that passed here read `assert 4 > 6000` in the clone, and iteration 155 was spent entirely as the hotfix. Iteration 155's FINAL lesson asks for this control verbatim: "COMMIT FIRST, THEN RUN BOTH EXTRA GATES IN THE COMMIT-TO-PUSH WINDOW -- IT IS THE ONLY POINT WHERE THE SHIP TREE EXISTS AND IS STILL LOCAL-ONLY." Cloning the LOCAL repo is what makes that window usable, and it also makes the check free to fail: a bad ship costs a `git reset` instead of a hotfix iteration. THE ONE JUDGEMENT CALL, AND IT REVERSES THE SCOUT: B1 proposed fail-CLOSED on BOTH exit 1 and exit 2, mirroring the leak-guard. The spec makes exit 2 ADVISORY, on a cost asymmetry the leak-guard does not share. A leak is IRREVERSIBLE once pushed, so blocking on 'the scanner could not run' is free; a ship broken only in a clone is RECOVERABLE, because `postrelease_step` still runs, raises `HOTFIX_NEEDED.md`, and the next PM's duty 0 repairs it -- the 154/155 sequence, cost one iteration. A false INCOMPLETE (a cold `uv sync`, a busy machine, an exhausted budget) would instead take the REVERT path and destroy a green iteration, which is the loss mode the standing operator directive is most emphatic about. So exit 1 is decisive evidence about the ship TREE and blocks; exit 2 is evidence about the MACHINE and reports. This is `postrelease_verdict`'s own principle (boundary failures are infra, never a hotfix) applied at the correct polarity for a gate. WHY A SIBLING AND NOT A `source=` PARAMETER, which would be fewer lines: `verify_fresh_clone` deliberately folds ANY seam exception to `PostReleaseResult(True, True, ...)` -- HEALTHY -- because a post-hoc reporter must never raise a false hotfix against an already-published commit. A pre-push gate has the OPPOSITE polarity, so inheriting that optimistic fold would make the gate bless a tree it never verified. An additive sibling keeps the RUNNING post-release semantics byte-identical, which is why behavior 12 makes 'no hunk in `postrelease_verdict` / `verify_fresh_clone` / `postrelease_step`' an assertion rather than an intention. TWO SEAMS THE FEATURE NEEDED ALREADY EXISTED, measured so the engineer adds no plumbing: `run_cmd` (foundry.py:7804) already takes `timeout: int = CMD_TIMEOUT` and folds a `TimeoutExpired` into `ok is False` with a non-empty `.out`, and `_monotonic` (foundry.py:7832) is already a patchable module seam documented as measurement-only. So the containment for the unbounded term -- `uv sync` in a cold clone -- is ONE module-level `PRESHIP_BUDGET_SECONDS` read at call time, with the remaining budget recomputed before each step and passed as that step's `timeout`, and the whole thing is scriptable offline by patching two names the repo already exposes. Reference figure for the default: iteration 155 ran this exact control BY HAND -- non-shallow local clone at the ship commit plus the full suite -- in 57.38 s warm. FOUR FACTS CHECKED SO NOBODY RE-DERIVES THEM: `cfg.setup_cmd` defaults to `"uv sync"` (foundry.py:224) and is a `str`, never `None`, so `_platform`'s config omitting it is harmless and `shlex.split` is safe; `cfg.state` is a PROPERTY (`work_root / "state"`), not a config field, and all four products keep `config.json` at `work_root/config.json`, which is what makes the card's `<state dir>/../../config.json` shortcut correct; `products/*/state/` is `.gitignore` line 2, so `cfg.state / "preship_clone"` owes no new ignore entry; and the stage PROMPT does not carry the config path at all (`build_prompt` emits repo, vision, roadmap, quality_ref, quality_bar, test_cmd, state dir and learnings -- no config), which is precisely why the card line must spell out the cwd-independent `python3 <checkout>/foundry.py preship --config <checkout>/products/<name>/config.json` form rather than a bare `--config <cfg>`. That last one is the same paper-cut roadmap item (h) closed for `roles/pm.md`, and it would have silently made this gate un-runnable at the one stage that needs it. WHY THE CARD IS THE WHOLE INTEGRATION: anything on the pipeline control path is INERT until the dispatcher restarts (item (j) -- the live brain is an Aug-5 process holding an Aug-5 `foundry.py`), while a CLI verb runs in a fresh `python3 foundry.py` and a `roles/*.md` card is re-read from disk EVERY stage. So one checklist line in `roles/final.md` beside the existing leak-guard step makes the gate live the moment it lands, with zero restart owed and zero resume risk. TRIAGED AGAINST scout A's A1 (`foundry steer`, a supported writer for the pinned operator steering head), the strongest alternative on either slate and the only candidate that changes what every stage in the fleet RECEIVES. It lost on its author's own two admissions: it is "a convenience over an edit the operator can already make by hand" whose value "rests entirely on the verification half", and it carries the largest blast radius on either slate because it writes a file every stage re-reads each round, so one malformed write degrades every prompt in the fleet at once. Taking that risk in the same iteration as a new ship gate was the wrong trade; A1 remains the top new-capability pick and its measurements stand (head parser at foundry.py:1304-1326, head 9,175 raw chars against the 10,000-char budget). ALSO OPEN, UNHARMED, with the scouts' own falsifications recorded so they are not re-proposed: scout B's B2 (roadmap item (s), a `tests/` lens banning ambient-tree COUNT preconditions) was falsified BY ITS OWN AUTHOR inside the scout stage -- a 30-line scan produced 6 findings across 137 test files and ALL SIX were FALSE, each naming the artifact only inside a docstring, a string literal or a `tmp_path` fixture builder -- so the live landmine count is ZERO and the candidate is purely PREVENTIVE, a weaker value claim than item (s)'s framing implies; an honest version needs an AST pass because the discriminator is 'a count whose subject flows from an untracked path', which is data-flow reasoning. Scout B's B3 (item (c), a checked per-iteration behavior-test precondition) also softened under its own measurement: 130 of 156 iterations have such a file and the roadmap's real evidence is just 2 iterations (121 and 125), and an agent reading a card cannot FORCE the file to exist, so it is advisory. Scout A's A2 (`foundry stop`) and A3 (`foundry resume-work`) both remain, A3 with the honest caveat its author supplied: the automated `capture_abort_patch` producer has never fired anywhere in the fleet and is itself inert until a dispatcher restart, so the verb would launch on a population of 3 hand-made patches. RIDER, and the first draft of it was WRONG in a way worth recording: the index grew 50,462 -> 51,144 chars on the first write, which left 2,856 of headroom against `ROADMAP_INDEX_HARD_CHARS` (54,000) -- i.e. INSIDE the 3,000-char `ROADMAP_INDEX_NEAR_WALL_CHARS` advisory band, while this bullet simultaneously claimed it was clear of it. Item (t) was then trimmed TWICE (682 -> 549 -> 522 chars of net index growth) to land at 50,984 with 3,016 of headroom, genuinely clear. Two transferable points: a docs-budget claim must be MEASURED after the write and not predicted before it, and the first trim (2,989) missed by 11 chars, so a near-wall margin needs a re-measure per attempt rather than one estimate. No existing clause was moved or re-worded, because every one carries literal strings pinned by tests. A compaction remains due within about three iterations and is a scheduling item, never a competitive candidate.**
- **iter 157 -- `build_prompt` emits ONE new `## Context` line naming this product's own config file, name-VERIFIED rather than merely conventional, and the two role cards that invoke `--config` verbs stop teaching path derivation (scout A's hardening/DX lens, candidate A2).** THE GAP, enumerated from source at head `9ba9b4b` rather than inherited: `build_prompt`'s Context block emits repo, push target, vision, roadmap, quality_ref, quality_bar, test_cmd, state dir, learnings, digest, iteration, output file and role file -- and NO config path, while almost every gate-bearing verb is a `--config <path>` verb. TWO LIVE CONSUMERS PAY FOR THAT TODAY, and one of them cannot resolve at all: `roles/pm.md:31` still reads `--config <this product's config>`, an unresolved placeholder with no derivation, and `roles/final.md:44` (shipped by iter 156) spends two clauses teaching it ("`<checkout>` is ... the PARENT of the `roles/` directory this card lives in ... `<this iteration's state dir>/../../config.json` names the same config"). The second is the pessimistic pre-ship gate, where a mis-derived path yields `PRESHIP: INCOMPLETE` (exit 2) -- a gate that silently stops gating -- so this is the class of paper-cut that turns into a safety loss, and it has now recurred twice at increasing depth (item (h) fixed the INTERPRETER, iter 156 hit the ARGUMENT). DESIGN CALL, and it is the whole reason this is not a one-line f-string change: `load_config(path)` KNOWS the real path and DISCARDS it (no `config_path` among the 19 `ProductConfig` fields), so the emitted line can only be a claim about a conventional location -- `<work_root>/config.json` -- and a stale file there would misname the product's config. Behavior 1 therefore requires the file to EXIST, parse as a JSON object, AND carry `"name" == cfg.name` before the line is emitted, which turns the claim from conventional into verified for the 90% case at zero schema cost; recording the authoritative path on the config object is named OUT OF SCOPE with its reason (it changes the dataclass field set that `unknown_config_keys` / `config_field_names` police and existing tests pin). BLOCKING acceptance criteria are deliberately the two that protect the fleet rather than the feature: behavior 6 (a product with no matching config.json gets a BYTE-IDENTICAL prompt for every stage) and behavior 2 (the helper is TOTAL -- missing work_root, a directory in place of the file, undecodable bytes, invalid JSON, a non-object document, a missing or wrongly-typed name all return None), because this edits the text every stage of every product reads. Blast radius measured, not assumed: no test pins any Context line's literal text (`rg` over `tests/` for the four Context labels returns nothing), iter-144's prompt-metrics test pins `(31, 7, 3)` on a SYNTHETIC prompt rather than the real one, and iter-106's byte-identity guard compares two `build_prompt` CALLS rather than a frozen string, so the insertion point (immediately after the Quality-check-command line, before the state-dir line) is free; the iter-142 brake `bare_foundry_cli_findings` over `foundry_cli_verbs(<source>)` is the live police on the two card edits and behavior 11 keeps it green. **SCOUT B'S TOP PICK B1 WAS DISQUALIFIED ON A FACT NEITHER SCOUT CHECKED, AND IT IS THE MOST TRANSFERABLE FINDING OF THIS ITERATION: `launch_dispatcher.sh` IS NOT TRACKED BY GIT.** `git ls-files --error-unmatch launch_dispatcher.sh` answers `did not match any file(s) known to git` and `git check-ignore -v` names `.git/info/exclude:8`, so the operator's canonical launcher is a machine-local file (it hard-codes an absolute checkout path under the operator's home directory and an absolute path to a locally installed application bundle). B1's finding is real and correctly reasoned -- the pre-launch guard IS fail-open (`pgrep ... >/dev/null 2>&1` makes a broken scan indistinguishable from 'nothing running', so it can launch a SECOND brain against a VISION hard constraint) and `running_dispatchers` was written precisely to separate those cases -- but editing that file ships NOTHING, and any test copying it out of the repo root would depend on gitignored local state, which is EXACTLY the iter-154 green-ship / post-release-BROKEN incident that iter 156's `preship` now catches from a fresh clone. Scout B verified `rg 'launch_dispatcher' tests/` and no freeze guard, and concluded editing it was legal; the missing check was `git ls-files`. THE SALVAGE, for a future iteration: ship a TRACKED, repo-agnostic launcher under `scripts/` (env-var configured, no machine paths, gating on `single-brain`'s 0 SAFE / 1 CONFLICT / 2 UNKNOWN, fail-CLOSED on 2) using the `tests/test_iter51_behavior.py` install_hooks.sh fixture shape; prefer `single-brain` over `preflight` there, because `preflight` folds in `run_doctor` and an env blocker is never downgraded, so on battery or a network hiccup it would turn today's fail-open into a fail-SHUT on the only durable launch path. OTHER SLATE ITEMS, dispositioned so none is re-proposed blind: scout A's A1 (a `roadmap-archive` move verb) is the strongest ALTERNATIVE on either slate and lost on two counts -- it is the same shape as iter 156 (another on-demand verb with no forced consumer, against the diversity guard) and shipping it BUYS NO HEADROOM, it spends some; A3 (preserve a timed-out attempt's partial output) was declined by its own author's ranking and by its own second objection (the 48-byte logs come from the CHILD's ~600s cap, which never raises `TimeoutExpired`, so the scoped fix does not close operator directive (3)); scout B's B2 (USAGE.md's cheat-sheet tells an adopter to `cat` a `STATUS_REPORT.md` that `find` proves has NEVER existed, `ProductConfig.report` having no writer) is the strongest surviving candidate for a later iteration and its brake is cheaper than its author thought, because iter 142 already shipped `foundry_cli_verbs` covering BOTH registration shapes; B3 (`live-lag --json`) stands as correct-but-consumerless. **INDEX BUDGET, and this iteration is the measurement that reframes the clock:** scout A rang a ~4-6 iteration alarm from 3,016 chars of headroom (50,984 against `ROADMAP_INDEX_HARD_CHARS` 54,000) at the observed +682 gross / +522 net of iter 156, and the index's own paragraph prices growth at ~988/iteration. But the LEDGER ROW is only ~114 chars: this iteration's total index footprint is ONE row (50,984 -> 51,098, delta 114), all detail living here in the archive instead. So the growth is dominated by PMs adding STATUS and item prose to the index, i.e. it is a DISCIPLINE, not a physical constant -- at ledger-row-only cost the wall is ~25 iterations out, not 4. Headroom is now 2,902, inside the advisory 3,000-char `ROADMAP_INDEX_NEAR_WALL_CHARS` band, so `foundry doctor`'s `roadmap-index:` line WILL warn: that is the designed signal and NOT a suite failure -- verified, not assumed, because every near-wall assertion in `tests/test_iter145_behavior.py` is monkeypatched and the two live index tests (`test_iter122_behavior.py:369`, `test_iter140_behavior.py:563`) check `over_budget` only. NO archive-move rider was attempted here, deliberately: under a ~600s stage cap a mid-paragraph split of the interleaved SHIPPED/OPEN STATUS blob is the kind of doc surgery that reds the suite for a docs-only reason and costs the whole iteration. - **iter 159 -- the composite test-quality scan stops doing the same work three times: `gather_test_quality` gathers the product's test-file list ONCE and opens a scoped tree cache for the duration of one composite gather, so each test file is read and `ast.parse`d ONCE instead of three times, and every emitted document is byte-identical (scout B's performance-and-throughput lens, candidates B1+B2 fused at their single shared call site).** THE MEASUREMENT, re-taken this iteration at head `66bb1f8` rather than inherited: on `_platform`'s 334 test files `gather_test_quality(cfg, files)` costs 3.387 s and `gather_test_quality(cfg, None)` 4.275 s, while ONE `ast.parse` pass over the SAME file list is 0.643 s -- so roughly 80% of the composite is re-parsing files two of the three lenses had already parsed, and a further 0.89 s is `_gather_weak_test_files` walking the repo three times (2 globs x 3 lenses = 6 rglobs) because `_company_rollup_cli` drives the per-product gather with the default `files=None`. Fleet-wide that is `company-test-quality` at 7.29 s, its live-smoke test at 7.53 s of a 69.86 s / 4,961-test suite, and the suite is subtracted from every stage's hard ~600 s cap. ADMISSIBILITY vs roadmap item (r): (r) de-lists iter 154's git-visible-snapshot fix as inapplicable to the four live-smoke tests and voids a ~35 s extrapolation -- and it NAMES per-verb AST scanning as their real cost, so attacking that cost with a fresh measurement is a new proposal, not the de-listed one; the scout flagged this and left the call to the lead, which is the right division. THE PRE-FLIGHT THAT CHOSE THE DESIGN: the obvious shape -- thread a `trees=` argument down from the composite into the three lens gathers -- is BLOCKED, because 17 monkeypatch sites across 6 test files replace those seams with `lambda cfg, files=None:` and would raise `TypeError` on an unexpected keyword the moment the composite passed one. Parse FAILURES are deliberately not cached, so an unparseable file is still attempted by each lens and each lens still records its own byte-identical `parse_errors` entry. RESIDUAL, pre-measured for whoever wants it: `test_quality_cli` still composes the three gathers inline, so the single-product `foundry test-quality` verb does not benefit until it is routed through this seam; and pruning the gitignored `products/*/state/` subtree (6,954 of 6,975 files under `products/`) from the walk is the bigger structural win but it changes what the verb scans, so it needs its own two-sided calibration.**
- **iter 160 -- the two docs every stage is pointed at stop pricing ONE retry ladder when the code has THREE: a pure `retry_ladder_lines()` derives each DISTINCT ladder by CALLING the live decision function `retry_delay`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`:90 and `CONTINUOUS.md`:39 carry the rendered lines, and a drift guard asserts whole-line PRESENCE in both files under arrow/whitespace normalisation (scout B's narrative-and-docs lens, candidate B1; closes roadmap item (l), open since 143).** THE MEASUREMENT, re-taken this iteration from the live function rather than inherited from the slate: `retry_delay` has three ladders -- `timeout`/`cli-error` = 60/120/240 s (1 -> 2 -> 4 min), `stalled` = 60/300/1200 s (1 -> 5 -> 20 min), `service`/`other` = 600/1200/2400 s (10 -> 20 -> 40 min) -- so the single documented `10 -> 20 -> 40` ladder is correct for 2 of 5 classified kinds and 10x WRONG on the first retry of the other three. It matters because `build_prompt` inlines `quality_bar`, which sends every stage to `ARCHITECTURE.md` for the invariants, and iteration 148 measured that the two mispriced ladders cover essentially all real failures of the two hottest stages (`engineer` {timeout: 19, cli-error: 1}, `tester` {stalled: 19, timeout: 7}). THE DESIGN CONTRIBUTION THAT CLOSED THE ITEM: 143, 148 and 154 all recommended this pick and all left the same blocker -- the corrected figures reduce to 1/2/4/5/10/20/40, integers that already occur throughout the docs (`ARCHITECTURE.md` alone holds 1 x7, 2 x8, 4 x4), so an integer-scanning guard is either noisy or vacuous. Both halves dissolve by deriving from BEHAVIOUR: render one formatted line per distinct ladder and assert that whole LINE is present, which cannot false-positive on an ambient integer and stays correct if a fourth ladder lands. THE HAZARD THE SLATE DID NOT STATE, found because the PM's own grep returned zero hits: both docs use the UNICODE arrow U+2192, not ASCII `->`, so any renderer or guard that assumes ASCII silently matches NOTHING -- the fail-open family the learnings head warns about. Normalisation folds U+2192 to ASCII on BOTH sides of every comparison and is load-bearing, not cosmetic. THE PRE-FLIGHT: swept the tree rather than trusting the scout's count -- 7 test files reference `ARCHITECTURE.md` (not 8) and 1 references `CONTINUOUS.md`, and NO test pins the stale ladder, proven two-sidedly by control-testing the same regex against the docs where it DOES fire; the one live constraint is `tests/test_iter149_behavior.py`'s `test_b9_live_bar_cites_the_real_resilience_invariant`, which asserts the whitespace-collapsed lowercased doc contains `resilience`, so the bold `**Resilience.**` name must survive the rewording. SCOPE DISCIPLINE: additive-dormant (no call site in `run_stage` / `run_iteration` / `build_prompt` / `dispatcher.py`, no config field, no new artifact, so no `.gitignore` entry and a live loop's resume semantics are byte-identical), and a `retry-ladder` CLI verb was considered and REFUSED because it would widen item (p)'s README-index gap from 2 verbs to 3 for zero decision value. TRIAGE, for the record: scout A's stage was KILLED (48-byte log) leaving three bare candidate titles marked `(refining)`; its A3 (`stage-times` in wasted agent-SECONDS) is the shape that normally beats a docs fix but was refused on measured blast radius -- 44 assertions in `tests/test_iter117_behavior.py` plus 22 in `tests/test_iter148_behavior.py` pin that output -- and A1 (share one AST parse across the 4 live-smoke tests) was refused on the diversity guard, being exactly iteration 159's shipped shape. RESIDUAL, pre-measured for whoever wants it: scout B's B3 (`ARCHITECTURE.md` sections 2/3 never name `preship`, the one gate that can BLOCK a push) survives untouched and is the recommended next docs pick; B2's README index is still short `new-product` and `preship`; and new item (u) records that the pinned `## Patterns` head sits 122 chars under its 10,000-char budget with nobody owning the pruning.**

- **iter 162 -- scout A's A1 bite 1: a `save-work` CLI verb that exports the uncommitted implementation to `IMPLEMENTATION.patch` in the newest `iter-NN` state dir, DORMANT (argparse-only; no role-card edit, no control-path call site).** WHY a second saver when `capture_abort_patch` (iter 151, `bc86d58`, 08-10 17:42) already writes exactly this patch: that one runs INSIDE `revert_repo`, i.e. inside the dispatcher's in-memory `foundry`, and the live brain (pid 61545) started 08-05 15:13 -- so it has never executed once. Measured by iteration 162's scout A: `find products -name 'ABORTED*'` is EMPTY fleet-wide while the four NIGHT_LOGs carry 20 `repo reverted to origin/<branch> (<stage> stage failed)` lines, i.e. 20 destructions, 0 rescues -- the newest being iteration 161's reviewed, fix-verified implementation, destroyed at 05:49:55 on 08-12. This is roadmap item (j) attacked from the side that needs NO dispatcher restart: a verb a role card tells a stage to run gets a brand-new interpreter, and cards are re-read from disk per stage, so both halves go live on the NEXT stage. DESIGN, with the index-safety proven by control test in a throwaway repo during the PM stage rather than assumed: the single I/O seam `worktree_patch_text` copies the index (located via `git rev-parse --git-path index`) to a temp file and runs `add -A -N` + `diff HEAD` with `GIT_INDEX_FILE` pointed at the COPY, so the real `.git/index` bytes and `git status --porcelain` are byte-identical afterwards and staged content stays staged -- which is why this must NOT reuse `capture_abort_patch`, whose docstring forbids a second call site precisely because its `add -A -N` mutates the real index and is only safe as the statement before a hard reset. It returns the diff's STDOUT ONLY (the shared `git()` helper concatenates stderr, which would corrupt a patch). Three further measured facts pinned by the spec: the patch survives the abort path because `products/*/state/` is gitignored and `git clean -fd` skips ignored paths (so NO new gitignore entry); a clean-tree run must leave an existing patch BYTE-UNCHANGED, because after the revert the tree IS clean and a re-run would otherwise destroy the rescue it just made; and the full rescue was verified end-to-end in the throwaway repo -- `reset --hard` + `clean -fd`, then `git apply --check` and `git apply` restored the modified tracked file, the untracked file and the staged file's content (staging itself is not restored, which is in Out of Scope). README gains the `# 46.` index entry -- MEASURED, not assumed: the entries run `# 0.` through `# 45.`, contiguous with no gaps and no duplicates, so the PM's own first draft saying `# 47.` was wrong and was corrected before hand-off. Triage: chosen over scout B's B1 (the `learnings-head:` headroom WARN, 122 chars of headroom against a 1,511-char median operator directive), because B1 makes a FUTURE loss visible while A1 stops a 20-times-measured one, and B1's own objection is that it fires permanently on the real tree and is clearable only by a human pruning a gitignored file. B1 is the recommended successor; scout A's A3 (verbatim `doctor stops:` retry) is the runner-up but its oracle is the one artifact iteration 161 never produced. NEXT: item (v), bite 2 -- the two role-card lines that make the rescue actually fire.**

Testing stages are 81% of all stage kills (59 of 73), which is why the checkpoint belongs at the END of the engineer stage, before the tester starts. WHY THIS BEAT SCOUT A's A1 (a near-wall WARN band for the pinned `## Patterns` steering head, measured at 122 chars of 10,000 headroom): A1 makes a FUTURE loss visible one bullet earlier; B1 stops a 20-times-measured one. This spec hit it, and the workaround was to hoist the quoted block into its own preceding section (0 -> 12 behaviors).

Of the 232, **223 were RESCUED** by the write-early checkpoint and **9 were LOST** (every loss an `engineer` attempt) -- split corrected at the fix-review gate, which caught that `produced` had assumed the output file is named after the stage label when `run_stage` takes `out_name` separately, inverting the `fix-review` and `fix-tests` rows from 100.0% rescued to 0.0%; **223 are <=60-byte stubs** with no transcript; by stage engineer 79, pm 58, tester 32, pm_scout_b 22, reviewer 17, pm_scout_a 15, final 4, fix-review 4, fix-tests 1. The blindness is BY CONSTRUCTION, not by accident: `StageAttempt` records `produced=True, kind=""` for a killed-but-checkpointed attempt (`foundry.py:11856`), so a stage killed at the 600s cap and saved is indistinguishable from one that finished naturally at 598s, and nothing in `foundry.py` reads an attempt log at all -- the only reference to that filename is the WRITE site (`foundry.py:15220`). The incumbent instrument cannot substitute: `_platform` medians are engineer 600.0s and pm 599.5s and **9 of 10 stages WARN over the 420s soft budget**, so that advisory is permanently on and carries no signal, the dead-advisory shape iteration 167's reviewer flagged. A monotonically growing file measured against a frozen ceiling is a scheduled revert, not a threshold: measured this stage the index is 51,992 chars, i.e. **8 chars** under the accepted 52,000 and **8 chars** over the accepted minimum headroom, so THIS iteration's mandatory row REDs the suite and reverts the iteration for a documentation-size figure -- whatever feature it carried. - **iter 173 -- the verb that measures the framework's one destructive failure counted 9 of its 64 losses, because its denominator holds work its numerator cannot see.** THE DEFECT, re-measured against this tree rather than inherited from the slate: `rescues` (iter 168, extended by iter 172's `kill_rate`) decides "this attempt lost work" from `ATTEMPT_KILL_TOKENS`, which holds ONE token -- the agent CLI's `agent run timed out after` -- so of the 64 `_platform` attempts on disk that produced no output file it counts **9 (14.1%)**. Percentages are deferred for the same reason -- counts only, deliberately, not forgotten. (2) `LossSummary.attempts` is a STORED field, the one place this class cannot mirror `RescueSummary`, which derives `attempts` from its rows. Here a row exists only for a LOSING kind, so an attempt that checkpointed its file has no row to be counted in; deriving `attempts` from `rows` would make it identical to `lost`, which collapses `exit_code` 0 (scanned, nothing lost) into an unreachable branch and makes the `lost <= attempts` invariant vacuous. - **iter 177 -- the suite is the one thing this product pays for three times an iteration, and it had grown 52.9% slower with no brake on it while the stage that runs it most sat at a 600.0s median with ZERO headroom.** WHAT SHIPS: `-n auto` in `pyproject.toml`'s `[tool.pytest.ini_options] addopts`, `pytest-xdist` declared in `[dependency-groups].dev`, a regenerated `uv.lock`, a new tracked `conftest.py` that sets `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` once per session, and one additive-dormant pure function `pytest_addopts_plugin_gaps(pyproject_text, group='dev')` whose only consumer is a suite brake asserting the real `pyproject.toml` has no gap. THE MEASUREMENT THAT PICKED IT, from `doctor` verbatim: `stage-budget: WARN -- 4/11 stage(s) within 60s of the 600s hard cap; worst is engineer at a 600.0s median (0.0s headroom, 11 no-output attempt(s) in 99)`. Scout B measured the suite at 71.21s serial vs 24.17s under `-n auto` (2.95x, 5752 passed, zero failures) and also caught that the serial number in roadmap item (k) was STALE BY 52.9% -- 46.58s when it was parked at iter 136, 71.21s now -- so the item had been costing more every iteration it stayed parked. WHY THIS IS THE ONE INCREMENT THAT GIVES BUDGET BACK: the suite is run by the engineer while iterating, by the tester, by the release gate, and again in both clone paths, so >=3 runs/iteration is ~214s -> ~73s, landing precisely in the two stages with the highest kill rates (engineer 62.1%, tester 26.1%). ITEM (k) HAD TWO STATED BLOCKERS AND BOTH WERE DISSOLVED RATHER THAN ACCEPTED. (1) 'it mutates the declared quality-check command the final gate and fresh clone consume' -- it does not, if the flag lives in `addopts`: `test_cmd` stays byte-identical at `uv run --with pytest pytest -q`, and BOTH clone paths (`foundry.py:8383` post-release remote clone, `foundry.py:8871` preship local clone) run `cfg.setup_cmd` FIRST, which is the `ProductConfig` default `uv sync` (`foundry.py:236`) because `_platform/config.json` sets no `setup_cmd` -- and `uv sync` installs the `dev` group by default. So `[dependency-groups].dev` is the ONE place all five consumers inherit the plugin, which is this repo's own rule about putting a fix where every call site inherits it rather than in one command. (2) '26 freeze guards would `git diff` from many workers on ONE tree' -- real, and a single green parallel run does NOT retire a lock race, as scout B's own objection says. The deterministic fix is git's documented `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`, set once at conftest import: it suppresses only the OPTIONAL on-disk index refresh and leaves `git diff`'s content comparison exactly as correct, and it covers all 26 guards AND every future git-invoking test without editing a single one of them -- where `--no-optional-locks` in argv would have meant 26 edits inside functions that `tests/test_control_path_freeze_scope.py` selects by their argv element literals. THE RESIDUAL CATASTROPHIC MODE, made impossible rather than merely avoided: if `addopts` ever requests `-n` while the plugin is absent, pytest fails at ARGUMENT-PARSE time, so every consumer reds at once -- including the post-release remote-clone verify, which raises `HOTFIX_NEEDED.md`. That is the exact shape of iteration 154's shipped-green / post-release-BROKEN loss and of roadmap item (s). So the pairing ships as a pure function plus an armed brake with a REQUIRED two-sided calibration (a doctored copy of the real `pyproject.toml` with the xdist requirement stripped must produce a non-empty gap list), because a green-only assertion proves the tokens are present and never that the assertion could fire -- the iter-176 tester lesson. Scout A's C1 (two flag helpers retiring 33 `--config` and 13 `--json` declarations, -123 lines) was rejected for this window specifically: 48 verbs on the running loop's CLI entry point is the wrong blast radius to combine with a change to how the suite is invoked, and it needs the full 48-verb `--help` golden diff as its gate. FOLLOW-UPS, deliberately NOT bundled: worker-count tuning (`-n auto` is what item (k) measured; `-n 4` and `--dist loadfile`/`loadscope` are a separate measured question), per-test `xdist_group` markers, scout B's B2 (the post-last-write tail is 23.56h, 3.4x the figure item (i)'s de-listing was argued on, and its concentration claim is INVERTED -- engineer+final are only 31.4%, while `pm` alone is 6.11h at a 117s median; the MEASUREMENT is in scope for a later shift and the early exit stays correctly de-listed, because much of that tail is the mandatory learnings/ledger duty), and scout B's C2 (byte accounting over the per-stage prompt, the most-repeated cost in the system and the only one never measured, but bytes are a proxy for tokens and tokens a weak proxy for latency). - **iter 183 -- the largest genuine duplication left in `foundry.py` was invisible to a line-ranked scan and obvious to a statement-ranked one: three test-quality scanners that are 13 statements each and differ in EXACTLY TWO tokens.** MEASURED, not estimated (re-derive with `ast.walk` over each `FunctionDef` body with the docstring node dropped): `gather_weak_tests` (L7610), `gather_constant_asserts` (L7846) and `gather_skipped_tests` (L8047) are 13 statement nodes each = 39 total, of which **26 are redundant**; the only differences are which detector parses each file (`find_assertionless_tests` / `find_constant_assert_tests` / `find_always_skipped_tests`) and which summarizer builds the frozen core (`summarize_weak_tests` / `summarize_constant_asserts` / `summarize_skipped_tests`). Everything else -- the `files is None` discovery branch calling `_gather_weak_test_files(cfg.repo)`, the `except (SyntaxError, OSError)` that appends `(str(path), f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}")` and CONTINUES, and the four-keyword `summarize_*` call -- is one graceful-degradation contract written three times, so a Behavior-15-class fix had to be made three times with nothing keeping the three in step. WHAT SHIPS: one private module-level `_gather_test_scan(cfg, files, detector, summarize)` holding that body once, and the three public `gather_*` reduced to a single `return` each with their signatures `(cfg, files=None)` and their own Behavior-naming docstrings intact. THE SHAPE IS PROVEN TWICE IN THIS FILE, so this is a pattern application rather than an invention: `_thin_gather_cli` (iter 165, L7654) collapsed eight per-product printers and `_company_rollup_cli` (iter 152, L9637) nine fleet roll-ups on exactly this axis. THE ONE RULE THAT MAKES IT SAFE, and the reason it is spelled out in the spec rather than left to taste: the detector and summarizer must be resolved by BARE NAME inside each wrapper at CALL time and passed in as arguments -- never a default argument, a module-scope `functools.partial`, or an import-time table, any of which freezes the patch site at import and silently stops existing `monkeypatch.setattr` tests from biting. Blast radius measured independently of the scout by regexing `setattr(<mod>, "<name>"` across all of `tests/`: 14 test files reference the triplet; the three public seams carry 22 monkeypatches (9 + 5 + 8) and are UNTOUCHED because they stay public functions; `_gather_weak_test_files` carries 5 and `test_tree` 0, both resolved by bare name INSIDE the shared body so they keep biting for free; and only **4 sites land on the inner names that must keep biting THROUGH the new helper** (`find_assertionless_tests` x1, `find_always_skipped_tests` x2, `summarize_weak_tests` x1). ## Compacted from the index by iter 182

### Migration note (per §6 self-mod guardrail) — iter 03
- **New sentinel introduced:** `POSTRELEASE: HEALTHY|BROKEN`, written to `products/<name>/state/iter-NN/postrelease.md` (last non-empty line). It does NOT participate in loop control flow — `run_iteration`/`run_continuous` branch only on `VERDICT:`/`RESULT:`/`ACTION:` and the `res["status"]` value; `POSTRELEASE:` is diagnostic and carried as an additive `res["postrelease"]` key.
- **New per-product artifact:** `products/<name>/HOTFIX_NEEDED.md` — a BROKEN post-release raises it (with the sha + evidence); a genuine-HEALTHY later ship clears it; the next PM must clear it before any new feature (now stated in `roles/pm.md`).
- **Unchanged:** iteration numbering, `state/iter-NN` layout, the `VERDICT:`/`RESULT:`/`ACTION:` sentinel strings, `run`/`once`/`doctor` CLI, and the `run_continuous` status branches {shipped, no-ship, infra-fail, stopped}.

### Migration note (per §6 self-mod guardrail) — iter 14
- **New per-product artifact:** `products/<name>/SPEED_STORY_NEEDED.md` — an **advisory** (NON-blocking) throughput flag raised on a genuine SLOW ship (`test_seconds > SUITE_SLOW_SECONDS`), auto-cleared on the next genuine fast ship, left untouched on infra-skip / disabled / errored. Git-ignored (`products/*/SPEED_STORY_NEEDED.md`).
- **New advisory PM duty** in `roles/pm.md` (after the hotfix check): consider a throughput/speed increment when the flag is present and no higher-value feature is warranted — subordinate to the blocking `HOTFIX_NEEDED.md` flag.
- **No new sentinel; ADDITIVE and off the control path:** `run_iteration`/`run_continuous` still branch only on `VERDICT:`/`RESULT:`/`ACTION:` and `res["status"]` ∈ {shipped, no-ship, infra-fail, stopped}; iteration numbering and `state/iter-NN` layout are unchanged. The advisory write/clear rides the existing post-release ship branch and is swallow-safe, so a live loop resumes cleanly on restart.

### Migration note (per §6 self-mod guardrail) — iter 26
- **No new sentinel; ADDITIVE schema enrichment only.** `events.jsonl` records gain one ADDITIVE `kind` field (a semantic type from the pure `classify_event`); the top-level `event` key stays `"log"` (backward-compatible — existing consumers filtering `event=="log"` keep working), and historical lines without `kind` remain valid JSON.
- **First change to `log()`'s emit call**, but strictly inside the EXISTING best-effort try/except mirror: the durable NIGHT_LOG write, all `VERDICT:`/`RESULT:`/`ACTION:`/`POSTRELEASE:` sentinels, iteration numbering, and `state/iter-NN` layout are UNCHANGED; `dispatcher.py` and every pipeline branch are untouched, and `kind` is never read on a control path — resume-safe (a live loop holds old code in memory; the change activates only on a clean restart). ARCHITECTURE.md §5 events bullet extended by one clause.

### Migration note (per §6 self-mod guardrail) — iter 52
- **No new sentinel; ADDITIVE gate-checklist step only.** `roles/final.md` gains a repo-agnostic leak-guard pre-push check (run `python3 <repo>/scripts/leak_guard.py --ref HEAD --repo <repo>` after the commit, before the push; a non-zero exit — 1 findings OR 2 error, fail-CLOSED — routes to the existing "If ANY fail — revert" path). It adds to the "ALL must hold to ship" set; it does NOT change iteration numbering, `state/iter-NN` layout, or the `VERDICT:`/`RESULT:`/`ACTION:`/`POSTRELEASE:` sentinel strings.
- **Resume-safe for an in-flight loop.** Role prompts are read fresh from disk each stage, so a running loop picks this up on its next `final` stage; the change is purely additive (can only ADD a revert-on-leak, never suppress a ship that would otherwise pass a clean scan) and produces the SAME ship/revert sentinels. `foundry.py` / `dispatcher.py` are byte-unchanged and every pipeline branch is untouched. Repo-agnostic: a product repo without `scripts/leak_guard.py` skips the check, so no other product's gate is affected.

- **iter 188 -- the tracked integration spec's own consumed contract was wrong in SIX ways, and because the seam it specifies is required to swallow EVERY exception, building it as written would have shipped a permanently-dormant feature that is indistinguishable from a correctly-dormant one -- this repo's own GAP-009 in miniature.** The pinned operator directive of 2026-08-16 ordered phase 1 of the `agent-gap-radar` integration (`docs/INTEGRATION_AGENT_GAP_RADAR.md`), additive-dormant, mirroring `pm_novelty_block`. Iteration 187 died `infra-fail` before triage, so it was still owed. MEASURED AGAINST THE PROVIDER'S BYTES, not the spec's table: (1) `radar` is NOT on PATH -- an uninstalled `[project.scripts]` console script; (2) `radar list` declares only `--floor` -- there is NO `--json` and NO `--layer`, and `--json` exists on `scan` ONLY, so the spec's primary consumed verb cannot be consumed as written; (3) the stored records carry NEITHER `priority` NOR `confidence` -- 15 keys appear in every record plus `check` in all but one, and both scores are DERIVED by `src/agent_gap_radar/scoring.py`; (4) `radar prd`'s positional is the REGISTER, not the target repo; (5) `import agent_gap_radar.registry` raises `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'pydantic'` under the ambient interpreter, so BOTH non-stdlib routes are closed and a CLI route would cost a `uv` subprocess inside prompt construction. CORRECTION 6 IS NEW THIS ITERATION AND IT DECIDES THE ORDERING RULE: `priority` is NOT `severity x frequency x tractability`. The integration spec says so in its own opening lines and iteration 188's scout A proposed ordering by that product; `scoring.py` actually computes a WEIGHTED SUM, `round(10.0 * (severity*3 + frequency*2 + tractability*1) / 40, 1)`. The two orderings genuinely INVERT on live records: GAP-005 `(3,3,5)` has product 45 against GAP-013 `(5,4,2)` product 40, so a product-ordering ranks 005 ABOVE 013, while radar's weighted sum scores them 20 and 25 and ranks 005 BELOW. So the obvious ordering rule would have made this repo silently disagree with `radar list` while appearing to agree. WHAT SHIPS is therefore a stdlib `json.loads` read of `<register>/gaps/*.json` -- offline, no subprocess, no `uv`, no PATH, no third-party import -- that carries the STORED fields verbatim and SYNTHESIZES NO SCORE AT ALL: it orders by the stored integers lexicographically and prints those three integers, so nothing here restates or competes with a derived figure the register owns and can retune. `gather_gaps` returns a plain dict `{register, records, unreadable}` rather than a new dataclass, and the `unreadable` count is the honesty half: a CONFIGURED register that yields zero records still renders a NON-EMPTY block, so 'no register configured' stays distinguishable from 'the register read is broken', which is the exact failure the swallow-every-exception clause would otherwise hide. TWO FURTHER LIVE FACTS THE SPEC'S PROSE GETS WRONG, recorded so a later phase does not inherit them: the status vocabulary is `("open", "partially-addressed", "addressed", "retired")`, not open/closed -- so the spec's phase-3 gate check 'status is `open`' would exclude GAP-016 `(5,4,4)`, one of the two joint-highest stored-score records -- and the register has GROWN from the 10 records the spec documents to 16, so nothing may pin a record count. MEASURED IN THE PM STAGE rather than inherited from the directive's own figure: over every `products/*/state/iter-*/final.md` on disk, exactly 5 iterations carry no usable ship token (`_platform` 118 and 123, `proactive-loop-agent` 130, 164 and 167); ALL FIVE attempt logs are byte-identical and unambiguous machine evidence -- the whole log reads `agent run failed: agent run timed out after 600s`, which is `run_stage`'s own timeout branch and not agent prose; ZERO are genuine refusals, because a completed gate that declines to ship writes `ACTION: REVERTED` and not one of these did; and FOUR carry an authoritative tester `RESULT: PASS` (118 via `tester2.md`, plus 123, 130, 164), so four green iterations at ~65 min each were destroyed by the conflation. TWO of the five wrote the banned `ACTION: PENDING`, which means this asymmetry is also what makes the PLACEHOLDER BAN safe: the digest already records that the ban itself then caused no-token reverts in 2 of 3 iterations, so the ban inverted the failure instead of removing it. Scope stated rather than hidden: only surviving state dirs were scanned, so 5 is a measured FLOOR from artifacts and not a contradiction of the directive's nine. WHAT SHIPS is one frozen `ShipDecision` (fields `verdict` + `detail` only; `ship`/`retry`/`revert`/`sentinel` are DERIVED properties so a caller can never hold flags that disagree with the verdict -- the `PreshipResult` idiom) plus one pure total `ship_decision(*, action, head_moved, attempt_killed, retries_remaining)` and the constant `SHIP_DECISION_TOKENS`, with ZERO call site. THE ORDERING IS THE WHOLE DESIGN: the ACTION token is authoritative whenever present, so an explicit `REVERTED` reverts even if the attempt was ALSO killed, and a `PUSHED` the branch head corroborates ships even if the attempt was killed; `attempt_killed` is consulted ONLY when the token is absent, and then it yields RETRY (machine evidence) unless the retry budget is exhausted, in which case today's REVERT stands. THE ASYMMETRY IS NOT INVENTED HERE -- it is generalized from `preship_verdict` (foundry.py:9089), whose own comment states the rule this repo already trusts: a GATE folds a boundary/seam failure to INCOMPLETE and only a REAL signal to BROKEN, because 'a false BROKEN would send a green iteration down the revert path'. A cap kill is a boundary failure; an `ACTION: REVERTED` is a real signal. THE BLAST RADIUS IS PINNED BY A TEST, not by prose: a TODAY-EQUIVALENCE oracle over the full 64-input cross-product (8 action spellings including `PENDING`, `MAYBE`, `""` and the lowercase `pushed`, times 8 boolean combinations) asserts `.ship` is True exactly where today's live rule `action == "PUSHED" and head_moved` is True, and that `.revert` diverges from today ONLY in the cells where the token is absent AND the attempt was killed AND retries remain. THREE DECISIONS WORTH THE READ. (1) The three new group fields sit BEFORE `kind_counts`, not after it, against the spec's word TRAILING and in favour of the spec's stated REASON for it: `tests/test_iter148_behavior.py:329` pins `fields(StageTimesGroup)[-1].name == "kind_counts"` AND its default, so appending would RED a test the engineer may not edit; a census of the two `StageTimesGroup(` construction sites (one all-keyword, one SEVEN-positional stopping before `kind_counts`) proved no argument could silently land on a different field. (2) `StageTimesSummary.cap` defaults to the literal `0`, not to `STAGE_HARD_CAP_SECONDS`: a dataclass default is evaluated at class-definition time and that constant is defined ~280 lines LATER, so naming it there `NameError`s on import; `render()` falls back through `getattr(self, "cap", 0) or STAGE_HARD_CAP_SECONDS` read at call time, which is the same read-at-call-time idiom that lets behavior 2's monkeypatch bite. (3) The `cap-hits ` fragment prints UNCONDITIONALLY, unlike `kinds `: a fragment that appears only when non-zero cannot be read as evidence of health, which is the whole defect this iteration exists to close. - **iter 192 -- the tracked integration spec promises that the gap seam degrades to `""` on ANY error, and that promise is FALSE for the most likely error there is -- a register directory that does not exist. Measured through the shipped code before speccing: an absent register renders a 423-char block announcing `0 record(s)`, not silence.** The falsehood is DELIBERATE and `gap_advice`'s own docstring says why: an UNCONFIGURED feed and a CONFIGURED-but-unreadable one are different facts, and collapsing them into one indistinguishable `""` is the fail-open shape this repo keeps rediscovering. That mattered because this iteration is the one that makes the block visible: on any machine without the sibling register the `pm` prompt now carries 423 chars of `0 records`, which reads like noise and invites exactly the tidy-up that would delete the only honest signal. So the spec PINS it as behavior 7 (an absent register must still announce itself and must NOT render silently) rather than leaving a well-meaning engineer or reviewer to "fix" it. General rule: when a doc states an invariant about a fail-soft path, EXERCISE the failing path and read what it actually returns -- a spec's claim about its own degradation is a claim, not a measurement, and the difference between `""` and `0 records` is the difference between a silent feed and a diagnosable one. **Second, on sizing a call site: 105 test files reference `build_prompt`, which reads as a huge blast radius for adding one line to it, but not one of them sets `gap_register`, so `pm_gap_block(cfg, stage)` returns `""` for every existing fixture and every existing prompt stays byte-identical BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by luck.** Price a proposed call site by what the callee RETURNS under the configurations that actually exist, never by how many files name the caller -- measured two-sided this stage at 0 chars with no register and 1,459 chars with the scoped one (16 records unscoped = 1,737; 5 scoped; all 5 non-`pm` stages 0). **Third, the triage: scout B's competing candidate (wire `ship_decision` at the release gate with `retries_remaining=False`) was beaten by its own measurement -- 0 divergences over all 374 `final.md` files this foundry has ever produced, with the RETRY cell unreachable, so it would not have recovered ONE of the 5 cap-killed iterations it prices.** It is the right next bite only when its retry budget arrives with it; an equivalence-only refactor at the one unrecoverable call site is the iterations 90-101 no-op shape wearing safer clothes, and it would have been a third consecutive bite in the ship-verdict subsystem (189, 191). Value per unit of RISK decided it: this feature fails harmlessly (the seam swallows every exception and `pm` is rescued on 100% of its kills), that one fails at the gate that destroys work it cannot recover. **INDEX PAYDOWN (fix pass): item (i)'s de-listing body is MOVED here verbatim so the index could shed 390 chars for the mandatory ledger row; the index keeps the label and the live do-not-re-propose clause.** MOVED BODY: (i) **DE-LISTED by iteration 130's scout A, measured -- do not re-propose:** cutting `MAX_ATTEMPTS` for the timeout kind (12 of 17 timeout stage-runs RECOVER on a retry, so it trades 5.83 h of visible waste for lost iterations); and an "output file is stable, so stop the attempt" early exit (the 7.01 h of post-last-write agent time is concentrated in engineer and final, whose real work lands in git rather than in the state file, so stopping there destroys code and commits).
